# dl-tool plan review — findings

Companion to [`PLAN-REVIEW.md`](PLAN-REVIEW.md), which explains the method and the patterns behind these findings.

> **Historical.** These findings were taken against `57d37de` and re-checked against `d8e81d3`. A second
> review pass on 2026-09-02 worked the plan and every critical finding below is now addressed, so a
> "Status: confirmed" line here describes the plan as it was, not as it is. Three of the claims below did
> not survive re-measurement — see [Second pass](PLAN-REVIEW.md#second-pass--2026-09-02). Check any finding
> against the current documents before acting on it.

**640 findings after de-duplication** — critical 25, high 110, medium 314, low 191.
Every critical and high finding was put through an independent adversarial verification pass whose
instruction was to refute it; 26 findings were refuted there and are not listed. Medium and low findings
were not verified individually and are listed in summary form in [part 2](#part-2--medium-and-low-findings).

| Area | Critical | High | Medium | Low | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| M0 — foundations, CI, config, store, auth, image, compose | 12 | 34 | 87 | 61 | 194 |
| M1 — task core and the aria2 engine | 7 | 33 | 58 | 24 | 122 |
| M2 — BitTorrent via qBittorrent | 0 | 12 | 29 | 16 | 57 |
| M3 — web UI | 1 | 10 | 43 | 16 | 70 |
| M4 — search | 2 | 3 | 18 | 31 | 54 |

| M6 — post-processing, scheduling, multi-user | 2 | 6 | 35 | 14 | 57 |

| Security and threat model | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 |
| Requirements and scope | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 |
| **Total** | **25** | **110** | **314** | **191** | **640** |

## Part 1 — critical and high findings

Each was confirmed by a verifier that re-opened the cited lines.


### M0 — foundations, CI, config, store, auth, image, compose

#### F001 · CRITICAL · Huma operations registered at /api/v1/... on the base mux while Servers URL and the TS client baseUrl already contain /api/v1 (prefix doubled)

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:67`, `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:69`, `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:70`, `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:111`, `docs/tasks/T014-typed-api-client.md:74`, `docs/tasks/T041-task-store-and-formatters.md:50`, `docs/tasks/T008-session-and-csrf-authentication.md:134`, `docs/tasks/T010-health-readiness-and-metrics.md:105`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T007: `root.Mount(cmp.Or(cfg.BasePath, "/"), base)` / `cfg2.Servers = []*huma.Server{{URL: cfg.BasePath + "/api/v1"}}` / `api := humachi.New(base, cfg2)` and step 3 `Register one placeholder operation, `GET /api/v1/system/info``. T014: `baseUrl: new URL(basePath() + '/api/v1/', document.baseURI).toString()`. T041: `paths['/sync']['get']['responses'][200]`. T008: `install `Authenticate` on the `/api/v1` sub-router only`; T010: `on the base sub-router, outside the `/api/v1` group`.
- **Impact:** With the Huma API created on `base` and operations registered as `/api/v1/system/info`, the generated OpenAPI `paths` keys are `/api/v1/...` while `servers[0].url` is `<base>/api/v1`, so the document describes `<base>/api/v1/api/v1/...`. openapi-fetch appends the schema path to `baseUrl`, so every SPA call goes to `<base>/api/v1/api/v1/...` and 404s at runtime while `make typecheck` passes. T041's `paths['/sync']` type does not exist at all (the key would be `/api/v1/sync`). T008/T010/T013 refer to an `/api/v1` sub-router/group that T007's router shape never creates; a chi `Route("/api/v1")` added later conflicts with the routes Huma already registered on `base`.
- **Suggested fix:** In T007's router snippet create the group explicitly: `v1 := chi.NewMux(); base.Mount("/api/v1", v1); api := humachi.New(v1, cfg2)`, keep `cfg2.Servers[0].URL = cfg.BasePath + "/api/v1"`, and change step 3, step 9 and the `TestOutsideBaseIs404` criterion to register/probe `/system/info` (relative to `/api/v1`). State in T007's interface contract that every Huma operation path is relative to `/api/v1` and expose the `v1` router on `Server` so T008 can install `Authenticate` on it and T010/T013 can register `/healthz`, `/readyz` and the SPA on `base`.

#### F005 · CRITICAL · `.env.example` ships an empty ARIA2_RPC_SECRET while compose.yaml uses `:?` on it, so the M0 checkpoint command and the `compose` CI job both fail at interpolation (adjacent to V1)

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:124`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:177`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:197`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:210`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:219`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:149`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:488`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:512`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T125:124 / doc 10:149: `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET: "${ARIA2_RPC_SECRET:?set ARIA2_RPC_SECRET in .env}"`. T125:177 / doc 10:488 (.env.example): `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET=`. doc 10:512: "`ARIA2_RPC_SECRET` uses the `:?` form in `compose.yaml`, so an empty value fails the `up` with a named error". T125:210: "fails with `set ARIA2_RPC_SECRET in .env` when that variable is unset". T125:219 (verification): `cp .env.example .env && ARIA2_RPC_SECRET=checkonly make compose-check`. Index:40: "Exit checkpoint: `cp .env.example .env && docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml up -d --build`"; index:42: "the `lint`, `test`, `gen-drift`, `compose` and `doclint` CI jobs are green". T002:78-80: the `compose` job runs `make compose-check && make docker-build` once `compose.yaml` exists, with no ARIA2_RPC_SECRET in the workflow.
- **Impact:** Compose interpolates the whole file before applying profiles, and `:?` fails on empty as well as unset. With `.env` copied from `.env.example`, `docker compose ... up -d --build` aborts with "required variable ARIA2_RPC_SECRET is missing a value" before any container starts, even though the aria2 profile is inactive. T125's own verification proves the author knows this (it exports `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET=checkonly` to run `make compose-check`). The `compose` CI job has no `.env` and no such variable, so `make compose-check` is red from T125 onward. Both halves of the M0 exit checkpoint (stack boots; `compose` job green) are unreachable, independently of V1.
- **Suggested fix:** In doc 10 §2 and T125's compose block use `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET: "${ARIA2_RPC_SECRET:-}"` and rely on T115's entrypoint refusal ("ARIA2_RPC_SECRET is empty; refusing to start an unauthenticated RPC endpoint", T115:55-58, restated at doc 10:459-460) as the guard; drop the `:?` sentence at doc 10:512 and rewrite T125:210 accordingly; change T125's Verification to `cp .env.example .env && make compose-check` with no override so the shipped defaults are what is actually tested. No CI change is then needed; if the `:?` is kept instead, T002 must set `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET` in the `compose` job and the M0 checkpoint command must be rewritten to generate it first.

#### F006 · CRITICAL · Default stack cannot boot even after V1: `.env.example` ships an empty QBT_PASSWORD while compose always sets DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL, which doc 11 makes fatal `config_missing`; qBittorrent's password is only knowable after its first start

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:72`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:74`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:180`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:97`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:491`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:272`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:499`, `docs/tasks/T005-config-loader-and-secrets.md:113`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T125:72-74 / doc 10:97-99: `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL: "http://qbittorrent:8080"` ... `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_PASSWORD: "${QBT_PASSWORD:-}"` (unconditional, profile-less). T125:180 / doc 10:491: `QBT_PASSWORD=`. doc 11:272: "Variable unset | `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_USERNAME` or `..._PASSWORD` while `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL` is set | Fatal. | `config_missing`". T005:113: "`config_missing` for the aria2 and qBittorrent credential pairs". doc 12:499: "from 4.6.1 it prints a random temporary password on each start instead". No document or task tells the operator how to obtain or fix a known qBittorrent WebUI password (grep over docs 06/10/11/17 for 'temporary password'/'WEBUI' finds nothing).
- **Impact:** Even with V1 fixed (aria2 URL empty by default), `config.Load` exits 1 with `config_missing` for the qBittorrent pair because `.env.example` leaves `QBT_PASSWORD` empty and the URL is always set. The operator cannot pre-fill the value: linuxserver/qbittorrent generates a random temporary password on first start and prints it to its own log, so a first `docker compose up` from `.env.example` always crash-loops dl-tool. The M0 checkpoint `curl -s localhost:8091/healthz` never answers.
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one and record it in doc 10 §6 and T125: (a) make the lane conditional — `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL: "${QBT_PASSWORD:+http://qbittorrent:8080}"` (same shape for aria2) so an unconfigured engine simply stays off; or (b) relax doc 11:272 to Warn + `engine_unreachable`/`engine_unavailable`, matching the existing "Engine URL unreachable at boot" row at doc 11:286 and doc 17 §1.5. Either way add the credential-bootstrap procedure that is missing today: a first-run step in doc 10 §6 and the README quickstart (temporarily publish the WebUI on `127.0.0.1:8080`, read the temporary password from `docker compose logs qbittorrent`, set a permanent one, copy it into `QBT_PASSWORD`) plus a "qBittorrent …

#### F007 · CRITICAL · Doc 10 §4 / T124 entrypoint say a non-writable data root is a warning; doc 11 §8 / T005 make it fatal — and on a fresh host the auto-created root-owned /srv/data triggers the fatal path

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:307`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:121`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:122`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:280`, `docs/tasks/T005-config-loader-and-secrets.md:114`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:249`, `docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:40`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 10:307: "Verify `/data` is writable as `$PUID:$PGID`; if not, log a `data_root_not_writable` warning and continue, so the UI can show the problem instead of the container crash-looping." T124:121-122: `su-exec "$PUID:$PGID" test -w "$root" || echo "entrypoint: data_root_not_writable $root as $PUID:$PGID" >&2`. doc 11:280: "Directory missing or not writable | any entry of `DLTOOL_DATA_ROOTS` | Fatal — a destination that cannot be written is not recoverable at runtime. | `config_path_unwritable`". T005:114: "`config_path_unwritable` after one `MkdirAll` attempt on `ConfigDir`, the database directory and each data root". doc 10:249-262 (Bootstrap) requires `mkdir -p "$DATA_DIR"...` and `sudo chown -R "$USER:$USER" "$DATA_DIR"` before the stack starts; the index:40 checkpoint command runs neither.
- **Impact:** Two authoritative documents prescribe opposite boot behaviour for the same condition, and the entrypoint's warning is immediately overridden by the binary exiting 1. Practically: `docker compose up` with the default `DATA_DIR=/srv/data` on a host where it does not exist makes Docker create it as root:root 0755; the process runs as 1000:1000, `MkdirAll` succeeds (dir exists) but the root is not writable, `config.Load` exits with `config_path_unwritable`, and the container crash-loops — exactly what doc 10 §4 step 6 says must not happen. The M0 checkpoint fails on any fresh host unless the undocumented-in-the-checkpoint bootstrap was run first.
- **Suggested fix:** Resolve the contradiction in one commit, either direction, and make the fresh-host path work: (a) preferred - change docs/11 §8 line 280 to Warn with `data_root_not_writable`, surfaced in `GET /system/info` and as a UI banner, with task creation into that root failing `path_rejected`, and drop data roots from T005 step 5's fatal `MkdirAll` list (keep ConfigDir and the DB directory fatal); or (b) keep it fatal and change doc 10 §4 step 6 plus T124 to exit non-zero with the same code, deleting the 'instead of the container crash-looping' rationale. Either way, add the §3.3 bootstrap (or a `mkdir -p && chown` line) to the M0 exit checkpoint in docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:40 and to T125's …

#### F008 · CRITICAL · No M1 task constructs the aria2 client, builds the engine registry or calls Connect()

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:172`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:34`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:54`, `docs/tasks/T027-list-and-test-engines.md:96`, `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:111`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:37`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:176`, `docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:72`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T019:172 'Do NOT wire the client into the HTTP server; T027 owns `GET /engines` and the registry wiring.' T019:54 'func New(cfg Config, hc *http.Client) (*Client, error)'. T027's Files table is settings.go, settings_test.go, store/settings.go, server.go and its steps only 'look each `kind` up in the registry' (T027:96) — no step calls aria2.New, engine.NewRegistry or Registry.Register. T020:111 'its constructor taking the store, the registry and the configured roots'. The first task that says where the registry is built is T029 (M2): 'Construct the client from config and register it in the engine registry' (T029:37) and 'Registry.Get("qbittorrent") returns the client after `NewServer` with a configured URL' (T029:176). grep over docs/tasks for engine.NewRegistry / aria2.New( / reg.Register finds no hit outside T019's own contract.
- **Impact:** Every M1 handler receives a registry that no task populates, so Registry.Get("aria2") is false for every HTTP URL, CreateTasks can only answer 503, GET /engines has no adapter to read capabilities from, and the M1 exit checkpoint 'A pasted HTTPS URL downloads to `/data`' (index:72) is unreachable. Nobody calls Engine.Connect either.
- **Suggested fix:** Add an explicit step to T027 (server.go is already in its Files table): in `NewServer` build `engine.NewRegistry()`, and when `cfg.Aria2URL` is non-empty construct `aria2.New(aria2.Config{URL: cfg.Aria2URL, Secret: cfg.Aria2Secret.Reveal(), Timeout: ...}, hc)`, call `Connect` logging `engine_unreachable` at warn on failure per doc 11 s8, `Register` it, and pass the registry to both `NewTaskHandlers` and `NewSettingsHandlers`; add an acceptance criterion `Registry.Get("aria2")` is true after `NewServer`. Repoint T019:172 at that step, and reword T029:176 as extending the same block.

#### F013 · CRITICAL · ErrNotFound is declared twice in package store (T006 db.go and T017 tasks.go) — compile error

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T006-sqlite-store-and-initial-migration.md:52`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:77`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:129`, `docs/14-conventions.md:82`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T006 (internal/store/db.go): `var ErrNotFound = errors.New("store: not found")`. T017 (internal/store/tasks.go): `var ErrNotFound = errors.New("store: task not found")` and step 2: "Create `internal/store/tasks.go` with `ErrNotFound`, `ErrIllegalTransition`, `TaskStore`". Doc 14: "Each package declares its sentinel errors at the top of its primary file."
- **Impact:** Both files are in package store. T017 must create tasks.go with a second `ErrNotFound`; db.go is not in T017's Files table so it cannot remove the first. The package fails to compile (`ErrNotFound redeclared in this block`), so T017's verification `make test PKG=./internal/store/...` cannot pass. T012 already relies on T006's `store.ErrNotFound` (ClaimJob returns it).
- **Suggested fix:** Delete the `var ErrNotFound` block from T017's interface contract (T017:76-77) and from step 2 (T017:129), replacing it with 'reuse the package-level ErrNotFound declared in db.go by T006; wrap it as fmt.Errorf("task %s: %w", id, ErrNotFound)'. Keep only `ErrIllegalTransition` as new in tasks.go, and change the doc 14 s2.2 snippet's comment so it no longer reads as a second declaration.

#### F016 · CRITICAL · Unwritable data root: doc 10/17 say the container keeps running, doc 11 §8 and T005 make it fatal; the image has no /data so unmounted runs (ADR-0011 confirmation, T124 acceptance) crash

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:307`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:66`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:280`, `docs/tasks/T005-config-loader-and-secrets.md:114`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:121`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:214`, `docs/decisions/0011-alpine-runtime-with-puid-pgid.md:70`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 10 §4 step 6 (line 307): "Verify `/data` is writable as `$PUID:$PGID`; if not, log a `data_root_not_writable` warning and continue, so the UI can show the problem instead of the container crash-looping." doc 17:66: "The container still starts so the UI can show the problem." doc 11 §8:280: "Directory missing or not writable | any entry of `DLTOOL_DATA_ROOTS` | Fatal — a destination that cannot be written is not recoverable at runtime. | `config_path_unwritable`". T005:114: "`config_path_unwritable` after one `MkdirAll` attempt on `ConfigDir`, the database directory and each data root". ADR-0011:70: `docker run -d --name dltool-uidcheck -e PUID=1234 -e PGID=1234 ghcr.io/l-k-m/dl-tool:dev && docker top dltool-uidcheck` (no mounts). T124:214: "`docker inspect --format '{{.State.Health.Status}}' <c>` reads `healthy` within 60 s of start."
- **Impact:** Two authoritative documents contradict on a boot behaviour T005 and T124 must implement. Following doc 11/T005, the entrypoint's warning is immediately followed by `config.Load` exiting 1, and `restart: unless-stopped` produces exactly the crash loop doc 10 promises to avoid. Because neither Dockerfile creates `/data`, a container run without a bind mount (ADR-0011's confirmation, any `docker run` smoke test) fails `MkdirAll("/data")` as the dropped user and exits, so `docker top` shows no process and the ADR's expected output is unreachable.
- **Suggested fix:** Doc 11 §8 owns the matrix, so settle it there: change the `DLTOOL_DATA_ROOTS` row to Warn + `data_root_not_writable`, mark the root unusable and surface it in `GET /system/info` and the UI banner (consistent with doc 10:307, doc 17:66 and the existing Warn rows for a missing binary and an unreachable engine), and change T005 step 5 to match while keeping `ConfigDir` and the database directory fatal. Independently, add `RUN mkdir -p /config /data` to T124's runtime stage (or `mkdir -p` each data root in the entrypoint while still root, before the drop) so ADR-0011:70 and any mount-less smoke test start.

#### F017 · CRITICAL · Generated api/openapi.json and web/src/api/schema.d.ts are never regenerated after M0, so the gen-drift gate fails from T020 on and T041's types do not exist

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:37`, `docs/tasks/T014-typed-api-client.md:106`, `docs/tasks/T041-task-store-and-formatters.md:49`, `docs/tasks/T041-task-store-and-formatters.md:50`, `docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:297`, `docs/tasks/T033-uploads-subfolder-and-selection.md:120`, `docs/tasks/T033-uploads-subfolder-and-selection.md:148`, `docs/14-conventions.md:229`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** Only T007 lists `api/openapi.json` and only T002/T014 list `web/src/api/schema.d.ts` in a Files table (grep over docs/tasks). T014:106: "`make gen` followed by `git diff --exit-code -- api/openapi.json web/src/api/schema.d.ts` exits 0, which is the same check the `gen-drift` CI job runs." T041:49-50: "export type Task = paths['/tasks/{id}']['get']['responses'][200]['content']['application/json']; export type SyncMessage = paths['/sync']['get']...". T020, T021, T025, T027, T046, T047, T050 (every endpoint-adding task) neither list the generated files nor run `make gen`; the one exception T033:120 says "run `make gen` and commit nothing else it changes" and :148 "plus `api/openapi.json` if `make gen` changed it" — still omitting `schema.d.ts`. Rule 1 (doc 14:229): "NEVER modify a file that is not listed in the current task file's `Files` table."
- **Impact:** After T020 adds POST /tasks the committed contract is stale: the blocking `gen-drift` CI job and the Go drift test fail for every later task, and at M3 `schema.d.ts` still describes only T007's `/system/info`, so `paths['/tasks/{id}']` and `paths['/sync']` are type errors and T041/T042/T044/T051 cannot pass `make typecheck`. An agent obeying rule 1 is blocked; one that regenerates violates the Files table (DoD rule 8).
- **Suggested fix:** State once in doc 13 §7.1 and doc 14 §7 rule 1 that `api/openapi.json` and `web/src/api/schema.d.ts` are implicitly part of the Files table of any task that registers or changes a Huma operation and must be regenerated with `make gen` in that task's commit; then add `make gen` to the Steps and the Verification block of T020, T021, T022, T023, T024, T025, T027, T031-T038, T046, T050, T092, T120 and the other endpoint tasks (T033 already does this and can stay as the worked example). Alternatively list both paths explicitly in each of those Files tables. Note the finding's doc 14 citation is off by two lines: rule 1 is at 14:227-228, not :229.

#### F021 · CRITICAL · Rules are creatable by any authenticated user, but grabs run as an undefined 'admin who owns the rule' — jail bypass, and no identity exists on the poll path

- **Where:** `docs/05-api-contract.md:199`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:201`, `docs/tasks/T071-commit-grabs-and-run-rule.md:119`, `docs/tasks/T071-commit-grabs-and-run-rule.md:102`, `docs/tasks/T071-commit-grabs-and-run-rule.md:50`, `docs/04-data-model.md:416`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:777`, `docs/tasks/T008-session-and-csrf-authentication.md:104`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 05:199 "| GET·POST·PATCH·DELETE | `/rules[/{id}]` | session\|token | Rule CRUD. |" and :201 "| POST | `/rules/{id}/run` | session\|token |". T071:119 "A rule-created task is owned by the admin who owns the rule; grabs count against that user's quota and concurrency limits exactly like a manual add." T071:102 "type ruleTaskCreator struct{ tasks *TaskHandlers; ownerID string }"; T071:50 "by calling TaskHandlers.CreateTasks". doc 04:416 `CREATE TABLE rules (id, name, enabled, priority, definition_json, last_match_at, created_at, updated_at)` — no owner column. doc 05:777 "`POST /tasks`, `PATCH /tasks/{id}` and `POST /watch-folders` reject a destination outside the caller's jail". T008:104 "func IdentityFrom(ctx context.Context) (Identity, bool)" is the only documented identity accessor; no injector exists.
- **Impact:** A non-admin can POST a rule with any `action.destination`; because `rules` has no owner, the only value `ownerID` can take is some admin, so the grab is created as that admin and the FR-124 jail and per-user storage quota are bypassed by design. On the poll path there is no request context at all: `IdentityFrom(ctx)` is false, so `TaskHandlers.CreateTasks` answers 401 and every automatic grab fails unless T071 fabricates an identity through an API T008 never exposes and T071 may not add.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `owner_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id)` to `rules` in doc 04 §3.5 (and to the M0 init migration via T006) plus `owner_id`/`owner_username` in doc 05 §10.2; set it from the creating identity in T068's CRUD. Validate `action.destination` against the rule owner's jail both at save time and again at grab time. Export `api.WithIdentity(ctx, Identity)` from T008 (add it to T008's interface contract and Files) — or give T071 a store-level creation path taking an explicit owner — so `ruleTaskCreator` runs the poll path as the rule owner instead of unauthenticated. If rules are meant to be admin-only, change doc 05 §2 to `admin` for all three rows and say so in doc 08.

#### F028 · CRITICAL · Default stack (no aria2 profile) hands dl-tool DLTOOL_ARIA2_URL with an empty secret, which doc 11 §8 makes fatal — the M0 exit checkpoint cannot boot

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:100`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:101`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:488`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:271`, `docs/tasks/T005-config-loader-and-secrets.md:110`, `docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:40`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** Doc 10:100-101 (dl-tool service, profile-less): "DLTOOL_ARIA2_URL: \"http://aria2:6800/jsonrpc\"\n DLTOOL_ARIA2_SECRET: \"${ARIA2_RPC_SECRET:-}\"". Doc 10:488 `.env.example`: "ARIA2_RPC_SECRET=" (empty). Doc 11:271: "Variable unset | `DLTOOL_ARIA2_SECRET` while `DLTOOL_ARIA2_URL` is set | Fatal. | `config_missing`". T005:110: "An empty string means unset, never an empty value." Task index M0 exit checkpoint (line 40): "`cp .env.example .env && docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml up -d --build` starts the stack; `curl -s localhost:8091/healthz` returns `{\"status\":\"ok\"}`".
- **Impact:** Following the plan literally, `docker compose up -d` without the aria2 profile (the documented default) gives dl-tool a set `DLTOOL_ARIA2_URL` and an empty (= unset) `DLTOOL_ARIA2_SECRET`, so `config.Load` exits 1 with `config_missing` and the container crash-loops. The M0 checkpoint and every later `compose up` based check are unreachable. (The same pattern applies to `QBT_PASSWORD=` with `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL` always set.) Doc 10's open question notes the profile problem but not this fatal interplay.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the URL follow the profile rather than being hard-coded: set `DLTOOL_ARIA2_URL: "${DLTOOL_ARIA2_URL:-}"` in doc 10 §2 and T125 (doc 11:79 already states an empty URL disables the aria2 lane with `engine_unavailable`), or ship `${ARIA2_RPC_SECRET:+http://aria2:6800/jsonrpc}`, and document that the aria2 lane is off by default. Apply the identical change to `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL`/`..._USERNAME`/`..._PASSWORD` or seed a non-empty `QBT_PASSWORD` (see F153, which owns the qBittorrent credential-provisioning half). Update T125's verification so it no longer masks the problem with `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET=checkonly`.

- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed

Doc 12 §6.5 says `Additionally allowed | the names the operator configures`. But `grep -rn 'ALLOWED_HOSTS' docs/` finds nothing, doc 11 §2 has no such variable, and T005's `type Config struct` (the authoritative field list) has no allowed-hosts field. T095's Files table is `internal/api/security.go`, `internal/api/security_test.go`, `deploy/caddy/Caddyfile.example`, `deploy/traefik/labels.md`, `internal/api/server.go` — it may not touch internal/config or doc 11.

The task cannot fix it inside its own Files table.

Also add the variable to the `.env.example`/compose environment block in doc 10 so the shipped proxy snippets work out of the box, and add a T095 test asserting a configured name passes.

#### F029 · HIGH · Huma's DefaultConfig schema-link transformer adds `$schema` (and a Link header) to every struct response body, so GET /sync cannot be byte-identical to the SSE `data:` payload

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:68`, `docs/tasks/T025-rid-deltas-over-sse.md:77`, `docs/tasks/T025-rid-deltas-over-sse.md:116`, `docs/tasks/T025-rid-deltas-over-sse.md:122`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:705`, `docs/decisions/0006-sse-with-rid-deltas.md:71`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T007: `cfg2 := huma.DefaultConfig("dl-tool", version)`. T025: `SyncOutput struct { Body sync.Delta }`, step 11 `asserting byte equality`, acceptance `The `GET /sync` body for a rid is byte-identical to the SSE `data:` payload for that rid`. doc 05 §6.2: `The response body is **byte-for-byte the same JSON object** as an SSE `data:` payload`.
- **Impact:** In Huma v2 `DefaultConfig` installs `NewSchemaLinkTransformer` through `CreateHooks`; at runtime it rewrites every struct response body whose schema is a `$ref` object into a copy with a leading `"$schema": "<scheme>://<host><base>/schemas/Delta.json"` member and appends a `Link: <...>; rel="describedBy"` header. The `/sync` body therefore carries `$schema` (and its value even depends on the Host header) while the SSE writer marshals the raw `Delta`, so `TestSSEAndSyncPayloadsAreIdentical` and ADR-0006's `TestSyncEndpointMatchesSSEEnvelope` fail as written; every Task/list response also gains an undocumented `$schema` member and the generated TS types gain `$schema?: string`.
- **Suggested fix:** In T007's router block, build the config without the link transformer — `cfg2.CreateHooks = nil; cfg2.Transformers = nil; cfg2.SchemasPath = ""` before `humachi.New` — and state in doc 05 §1.1/§1.3 that no response carries a `$schema` member (which also settles the unverified note at 05:118). If the transformer is deliberately kept, doc 05 §6.2 must drop 'byte-for-byte' and T025 step 11 must compare after stripping `$schema`.

#### F031 · HIGH · GET /events and GET /sync are not owner-filtered: the shared hub fans every user's task deltas to every subscriber, contradicting doc 05 §1.2 and FR-119

- **Where:** `docs/05-api-contract.md:68`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:176`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:280`, `docs/02-requirements.md:780`, `docs/tasks/T085-ownership-filtering-and-quotas.md:16`, `docs/tasks/T085-ownership-filtering-and-quotas.md:92`, `docs/tasks/T011-sse-hub-and-rid-ring.md:48`, `docs/tasks/T025-rid-deltas-over-sse.md:77`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 05 §1.2: `Non-admins act on their own tasks only; another user's task returns `404`, never `403``; §2 lists `GET /events` and `GET /sync` as task read endpoints for `session|token`. FR-119: `dl-tool shall return only the tasks owned by that user from every task read endpoint`. T085 goal: `A non-admin sees only their own tasks from every task read endpoint` but its steps only touch `Get`, `ListFiles`, `ListEvents`, trackers and peers reads`. T011/T025: one `Ring`, `Publish ... fans it out`, `Tasks map[string]json.RawMessage`, no identity anywhere; a delta task object `carries **only the fields that changed**`.
- **Impact:** Every non-admin who opens the SPA receives, over SSE and /sync, the name, destination, source_uri, progress and removal of every other user's tasks, plus global `stats`. That is a cross-tenant information disclosure and it also makes the client-side grid show rows the REST list never returned. Because partial deltas do not carry `owner_id`, filtering cannot be bolted on at send time without an owner index; no task owns this.
- **Suggested fix:** State in doc 05 §6.1 that `/events` and `/sync` are owner-scoped exactly like `GET /tasks`. Give the hub a task-id -> owner-id index maintained from each `Snapshot`, make `Subscribe`/`Snapshot` take a `Scope` (`Subscribe(ctx, scope, lastEventID, …)`) in T025's contract, filter `tasks`/`tasks_removed` and recompute `stats` over the caller's subset, and add `internal/api/sse.go` + a `TestNonAdminStreamSeesOwnTasksOnly` case to T085's Files, steps and acceptance criteria.

#### F032 · HIGH · T007 installs chi `middleware.RealIP` unconditionally, so X-Forwarded-For from any client is trusted, defeating DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES and the per-IP login throttle

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:65`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:72`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:594`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:480`, `docs/tasks/T009-first-run-setup-and-login.md:110`, `docs/tasks/T005-config-loader-and-secrets.md:69`, `docs/tasks/T095-proxy-hardening-and-headers.md:48`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T007: `root.Use(middleware.RequestID, middleware.RealIP, middleware.Recoverer, requestLogger(log))`. doc 11: `DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES ... Sources whose `X-Forwarded-For` and `X-Forwarded-Proto` are honoured. Empty means no forwarded header is trusted and the peer address is used.` doc 10: `Honour `X-Forwarded-Proto`, `-Host` and `-For` only from `DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES``. doc 12 §6.3: `peer address (or the `X-Forwarded-For` entry from a trusted proxy, never a forged one)`. T009: `a per-source-IP bucket of 10 attempts per 5 minutes`.
- **Impact:** chi's `RealIP` rewrites `r.RemoteAddr` from `True-Client-IP`, `X-Real-IP` or the first `X-Forwarded-For` entry regardless of who sent it. T009's per-IP bucket and every log record then key on an attacker-chosen value: rotating the header bypasses the 10-per-5-minutes limit for password spraying, and log correlation is forged. T005 already parses `TrustedProxies []netip.Prefix` but T007 never uses it, and T095 (M7) only covers `X-Forwarded-Proto` for the cookie `Secure` flag.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace `middleware.RealIP` in T007's chain with a `clientIP(cfg.TrustedProxies)` middleware that uses the TCP peer unless the peer is inside a configured CIDR, in which case it takes the right-most non-trusted `X-Forwarded-For` hop; add a T007 test asserting a forged `X-Forwarded-For`/`X-Real-IP` from an untrusted peer is ignored; note in T009 that the login bucket and the fail2ban log field use that resolved address, and reference the same helper from T095:48 for `X-Forwarded-Proto`/`X-Forwarded-Host`.

- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T007 step 9: `a request to `<base>/api/v1/system/info` returns 200`; acceptance `TestBasePathMountsEverything` passes for `BasePath` empty and for `/dl-tool``. T008 Files: `internal/api/server.go | edit` and `No other file may be modified`; step 9: `install `Authenticate` on the `/api/v1` sub-router only`. T009 Files also exclude `server_test.go`. T013: ``GET /dl-tool/api/v1/system/info` still reaches the API`. T092: `Add `GET /system/info` to T091's file` and `Restrict `GET /system/info` to admins`.
- **Impact:** From T008 onward `GET /api/v1/system/info` without a cookie is 401, so T008's own verification (`make test PKG=./internal/api/...`) fails on T007's test and the agent is forbidden to touch it; after T009 the same request is 401 `/problems/setup-required` until a user exists, so T013's `TestAPIRouteNotShadowed` also cannot get JSON task data. T092 then registers a second `GET /system/info` over T007's placeholder in the same Huma API (duplicate route/operation) and makes it admin-only, so the only unauthenticated smoke route the M0 tests rely on disappears without any task updating those tests.

#### F046 · HIGH · Makefile installs golangci-lint from the v1-only module path; the v1 line ends at v1.64.8 and cannot analyse Go 1.26, so `make setup`/`make lint` cannot both succeed; and no `.golangci.yml` enforces the doc-comment rule

- **Where:** `docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:56`, `docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:66`, `docs/tasks/T001-makefile-and-doclint.md:48`, `docs/tasks/T001-makefile-and-doclint.md:92`, `docs/14-conventions.md:78`, `docs/tasks/T123-ssrf-guarded-http-client.md:169`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 13:56: `$(GO) install github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/cmd/golangci-lint@$(GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION)`; doc 13:66: `golangci-lint run ./...`. T001:48: `GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION ?= PINME # pin at implementation time`; T001:92: "`GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION` and `LYCHEE_VERSION` both hold a real version string". Live check (proxy.golang.org, this review): `github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/@v/list` ends at v1.64.8; `github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2/@v/list` reaches v2.13.2. doc 14:78: "An exported symbol with no doc comment fails `make lint`." T123:169: "Do NOT add a repository-wide `depguard` rule or a `.golangci.yml`" — no task's Files table contains a `.golangci.yml`.
- **Impact:** Any v2.x pin fails `go install` ("module path must match major version (github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2)"); the only versions the path accepts are v1.x, and v1.64.8 (built with Go 1.24) refuses a `go 1.26` module ("the Go language version used to build golangci-lint is lower than the targeted Go version"). So the `lint` job in the M0 exit checkpoint cannot be green with the Makefile as written, and DoD rule 1 (`make lint` exits 0) is unsatisfiable from T004 onward. Separately, with no `.golangci.yml`, golangci-lint's defaults do not check doc comments, so doc 14 §2.1's stated enforcement does not exist.
- **Suggested fix:** Change the install line in doc 13 §2 and T001's Makefile head to `github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2/cmd/golangci-lint@$(GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION)` and pin a v2.x release (v2.13.2 resolves today); note in T001 that the v1 module path is dead at v1.64.8. Then either add `.golangci.yml` to T001's Files table enabling a doc-comment linter (`revive`'s `exported` rule or `staticcheck` ST1000/ST1020, which also needs v2's config schema) or delete the claim at doc 14:78.

#### F080 · HIGH · T060's acceptance `go list -deps ./internal/search` contains no os/exec fails: internal/search imports internal/store (T057 SeedIndexers), and modernc.org/libc's linux build imports os/exec

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T060-qbittorrent-plugin-metadata-import.md:106`, `docs/tasks/T057-bundled-engine-definitions.md:84`, `docs/14-conventions.md:118`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T060 acceptance: "`go list -deps ./internal/search` contains no `os/exec` and no `plugin`." T057 contract (package search): `func (r *Registry) SeedIndexers(ctx context.Context, s *store.IndexerStore) (created int, err error)`. Doc 14 §2.4: the query layer is `modernc.org/sqlite v1.57.0`. Verified in the local module cache: `modernc.org/libc@v1.74.4/libc_musl.go` (build tag `linux && (amd64 || arm64 || ...)`) imports `"os/exec"`; modernc.org/sqlite depends on modernc.org/libc.
- **Impact:** `go list -deps` is transitive. Because T057 makes `internal/search` import `internal/store`, which imports modernc.org/sqlite -> modernc.org/libc, `os/exec` is in the dependency set on every Linux build. The T060 agent cannot satisfy the criterion without breaking the T057 import (not in its Files) and, by hard rule 2, may not weaken the assertion, so the task stops at Blocked.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite the T060 criterion as a direct-import assertion, e.g. '`go list -f "{{join .Imports \"\\n\"}}" ./internal/search` contains no `os/exec` and no `plugin`', which is what T059's `TestNoInterpreterIsSpawned` (T059:132) already checks by parsing the package's own files; keep the same wording in T058's acceptance line 173 and T059's line 151 so the three tasks assert the same thing.

#### F084 · HIGH · The M5 exit-criterion feed routes to aria2, not qBittorrent: Arch's x-bittorrent enclosure URL ends in /torrent/, and T069 derives the engine from uri.Normalize + engine.Route, discarding the tier-A type

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T069-rule-matching-algorithm.md:156`, `docs/tasks/T069-rule-matching-algorithm.md:82`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:976`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:267`, `docs/tasks/T016-engine-interface-and-router.md:75`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:515`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:483`, `docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:166`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T069:156 "Set `Candidate.Engine` from `uri.Normalize(item.download_url)` followed by `engine.Route(n, nil)`, so a rule lands HTTP items on aria2 and magnets on qBittorrent exactly as a pasted URI would."; doc 05:976 `"download_url":"https://archlinux.org/releng/releases/2026.09.01/torrent/"`; doc 06:267 "| 2 | `magnet:?…`, a URL whose path ends `.torrent`, raw `.torrent` bytes, a bare 40-hex or 64-hex infohash | `qbittorrent` |"; doc 08:515 feed 1 "Extraction ladder tier A; the M5 exit criterion feed and the happy-path fixture."; index M5 "Exit checkpoint: A rule auto-downloads a release feed". doc 06 §4.1 daemon flags contain no `--follow-torrent`.
- **Impact:** Arch's enclosure URL (and DistroWatch/Torznab enclosures whose path does not end in `.torrent`) normalises to KindHTTP and Route row 4 returns aria2. The parser knew the enclosure was `application/x-bittorrent` (tier A) but neither feed_items nor Candidate carries that fact, so the rule hands a .torrent URL to aria2 as a plain HTTP download. With aria2's default `--follow-torrent=true` the BitTorrent transfer then runs inside aria2, outside the qBittorrent adapter, contradicting engine ownership; with it off, the user gets a downloaded .torrent file. Either way the M5 checkpoint ("a rule auto-downloads a release feed") is not met for the feed the plan names as its exit criterion.
- **Suggested fix:** Carry the extraction tier (or an `is_torrent` flag) out of `ExtractDownloadURI` into `Candidate`, and have T069 set `n.Kind = uri.KindTorrent` before `engine.Route` whenever the URI came from tier A/B/C so torrent URIs route to qbittorrent regardless of path suffix; add a T069 test using the Arch enclosure URL, and state the same rule for `POST /tasks` submissions of content-typed torrent URLs.

- **Evidence:** NFR-004: "stop accepting new work, flush task state, run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`, close the database and exit 0 within 20 seconds"; Verify: "T124 sends SIGTERM under load and asserts exit code 0 within 20 seconds and that no `-wal` file remains." Doc 17 §2 lists the six-step handler. T124 Files: `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `deploy/entrypoint.sh`, and `cmd/dl-tool/main.go` "edit | Consume the `serve` verb and add the `healthcheck` verb"; its acceptance is only "`docker stop <c>` returns within 20 s and the container exit code is `0`". T004 leaves `hooks.OnStop(func() { /* T007 shuts it down here */ })`. `wal_checkpoint` appears in no docs/tasks/T*.md.
- **Impact:** No task closes the store or checkpoints the WAL on SIGTERM, so a graceful stop leaves `dl-tool.db-wal`/`-shm` behind, which doc 17 §2 and doc 04 §6 say marks a killed shutdown and makes hand copies unsafe. NFR-004's own verification is never executed as written.
- **Suggested fix:** Give doc 17 §2's six ordered steps an owner: add `Close()` to T006's interface contract (`PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` then `db.Close()`) with a `db_test.go` case asserting no `-wal`/`-shm` file remains; add a `MarkNotReady`/drain step to T010 so `/readyz` answers 503 first; have T012 cancel the scheduler and workers; and put the ordered `OnStop` sequence plus the "no `-wal` after SIGTERM" assertion into T124's steps and acceptance criteria, since doc 02:992 names T124 as the verifier.

Doc 09 §9 BitTorrent row lists "DHT · PeX · LSD · Encryption (Disable / Auto / Always) · Maximum peers per task · Default share-ratio limit ...". T119 Goal: "`/settings/bittorrent` renders Download Station's BitTorrent row read-only, naming for each control where it is actually set" and "Do NOT proxy qBittorrent preferences ... DHT, PeX, LSD, encryption and max-peers stay in that daemon's own UI." T125 compose: "WebUI is deliberately NOT published: only dl-tool talks to it." Doc 11 §5 has no key for any of them and doc 02 has no FR.
- **Impact:** A parity row marked `parity` is unimplemented, and the place T119 sends the user (qBittorrent's WebUI) is unreachable in the shipped stack, so these settings require editing compose or qBittorrent.conf — exactly what the quality goal forbids. The 'centrepiece' UI and the vision document disagree with the task.

#### F099 · HIGH · Entrypoints write /etc/localtime and /etc/timezone before the non-root check under `set -eu`, so the documented `user:` path aborts

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:90`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:96`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:102`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:205`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:213`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:300`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:331`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:61`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T124 entrypoint: line 90 `set -eu`; line 96 `ln -snf "/usr/share/zoneinfo/$TZ" /etc/localtime` and `printf '%s\n' "$TZ" > /etc/timezone` run first; only at line 102 `if [ "$(id -u)" -ne 0 ]; then ... exec /usr/local/bin/dl-tool "$@"`. doc 10 §4.1:331: "The entrypoint then detects a non-root UID, skips user creation and `chown`, still applies `UMASK` and `TZ`, and `exec`s the binary directly." T124 step 8 (205): "Run it again with `--user 1000:1000` and confirm the entrypoint logs the already-non-root line ... and still starts"; acceptance 213. T115 entrypoint repeats the pattern (line 61 `ln -snf ...`, line 68 `: > /config/aria2.conf`) before its root check.
- **Impact:** `/etc` is root-owned 0755; as uid 1000 `ln -snf` into it and the redirect to `/etc/timezone` return EACCES, and `set -e` terminates the script before the non-root branch is reached. The `user:` alternative (doc 10 §4.1), T124 step 8 and acceptance criterion line 213 are unreachable as written; the same applies to the aria2 image under `user:`. (`/etc/localtime` need not be rewritten at all: Go, yt-dlp and aria2c honour the `TZ` environment variable via tzdata.)
- **Suggested fix:** In both entrypoints move the identity branch above the time-zone block, or wrap the block as `if [ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] && [ -f "/usr/share/zoneinfo/$TZ" ]; then ln -snf ...; printf ... ; fi`, keeping `umask "$UMASK"` unconditional; in T115 guard `mkdir -p /config`, `: > /config/aria2.conf` and `touch /config/aria2.session` the same way. Amend doc 10 §4 step 2 to say the symlink is written only when the entrypoint runs as root and that `TZ` is otherwise honoured straight from the environment by Go, aria2c and yt-dlp.

#### F105 · HIGH · e2e:server overwrites the tracked internal/api/dist/index.html placeholder, leaving the tree dirty and breaking DoD rule 8 for T043, T103, T104

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T043-playwright-harness-and-grid-performance.md:92`, `docs/tasks/T043-playwright-harness-and-grid-performance.md:121`, `docs/tasks/T013-embed-spa-and-base-path.md:82`, `docs/tasks/T013-embed-spa-and-base-path.md:131`, `docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:143`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T043:92: `"e2e:server": "... npm run build && rm -rf ../internal/api/dist && cp -R dist ../internal/api/dist && cd .. && go build -o bin/dl-tool ./cmd/dl-tool && exec ./bin/dl-tool"`. T043:121: "The run leaves nothing inside the repository: the state directory is under the OS temp directory." T013:82: "Edit `.gitignore` to add `internal/api/dist/*` followed by `!internal/api/dist/index.html`"; T013:131: "Do NOT commit a real `dist/` build output; only the placeholder `index.html` is committed." doc 13 DoD 8: git status must match "the task's `## Files` table exactly — no extra path, no missing path."
- **Impact:** `internal/api/dist/index.html` is tracked and un-ignored, so every `make e2e` run replaces the placeholder with the Vite build and `git status` shows it modified. T043/T103/T104 (and any later task running `make e2e`) cannot satisfy the scope check without either committing a real build output (forbidden by T013) or an undocumented `git checkout` step.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the placeholder out of the copy target: either have `e2e:server` build into a temp dir and `go build` with the real `dist` restored afterwards (`git checkout -- internal/api/dist/index.html` appended to the script), or make the embed directory fully ignored and generate the placeholder in the Makefile. Whichever is chosen, state it in doc 13 §2 next to the `e2e` target and repeat the exemption in the scope-check note of T043, T064, T103 and T104.

#### F130 · HIGH · FR-010 (FTP directory recursion) has no mechanism, no task steps and no FTP client: aria2 cannot enumerate a remote directory

- **Where:** `docs/02-requirements.md:148`, `docs/01-vision-and-scope.md:54`, `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:11`, `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:124`, `docs/tasks/T004-go-module-and-entrypoint.md:48`, `docs/tasks/T004-go-module-and-entrypoint.md:64`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:549`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** FR-010: 'When a submitted `ftp://` URI ends with `/`, dl-tool shall enumerate the directory and create one child transfer per file and sub-directory it contains.' Verify: 'T020 points at a fixture FTP server directory with two files and asserts two child transfers.' doc 01 §3: 'FTP directory recursion | Trailing `/` on an `ftp://` link downloads the folder ... | parity | Same rule, same restriction'. T020 Implements lists FR-010, but its only FTP step is '9. Pass `ftp_credentials` to the adapter through `engine.AddRequest.Extra`'; no step, acceptance criterion or test mentions directory enumeration or child transfers. T004's go.mod list (huma, chi, go-cmp, sqlx, tint, goose, client_golang, cron, testify, testcontainers, modernc sqlite, ulid, x/crypto, yaml, gofeed, goquery, anacrolix/torrent) contains no FTP client. doc 06 §4 maps `Add` to `aria2.addUri` per URI and has no FTP directory …
- **Impact:** aria2 downloads exactly the URI it is given; it has no directory-listing or recursive-FTP mode, so nothing in the plan can produce 'one child transfer per file'. An agent doing T020 either silently skips FR-010 (its acceptance criteria never mention it) or has to add an FTP LIST client and a parent/child task model that no document (04, 05, 06) defines. The doc 01 'parity' claim is unbuilt.
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one: (a) demote FR-010 to deferred in doc 02 and the deferral register, change the doc 01 row from 'parity' to 'deferred to v2', and drop FR-010 from T020's Implements; or (b) give it its own task with an ADR for a Go FTP client (e.g. jlaffaye/ftp), a parent/child column in doc 04 §3.3 and a response shape in doc 05 §5.2, and remove it from T020. Note FR-010's priority is 'should', so (a) is the cheaper resolution.

#### F141 · HIGH · No task implements DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES; T007 installs chi middleware.RealIP unconditionally, so forwarded headers are trusted from anyone

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T007-http-server-and-openapi.md:65`, `docs/tasks/T005-config-loader-and-secrets.md:69`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:72`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:480`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:445`, `docs/tasks/T009-first-run-setup-and-login.md:110`, `docs/tasks/T095-proxy-hardening-and-headers.md:48`, `docs/tasks/T095-proxy-hardening-and-headers.md:110`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T007: `root.Use(middleware.RequestID, middleware.RealIP, middleware.Recoverer, requestLogger(log))`. doc 11: "`DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES` ... Sources whose `X-Forwarded-For` and `X-Forwarded-Proto` are honoured. Empty means no forwarded header is trusted and the peer address is used. | `internal/api/server.go`". T005 parses it (`TrustedProxies []netip.Prefix`) but no task consumes it (grep: only T005 and a comment in T095). doc 12 §6.3: "keyed on the peer address (or the `X-Forwarded-For` entry from a trusted proxy, never a forged one)". doc 12 §6.1: "`Secure` set whenever the request arrived over TLS, judged from the listener or from `X-Forwarded-Proto` sent by a `DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES` peer". T095: "decided from X-Forwarded-Proto when the peer is in DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES" (no helper named, no file owns it). T008 cookie contract: `[; Secure]` with no rule for deciding it.
- **Impact:** chi's RealIP rewrites r.RemoteAddr from True-Client-IP / X-Real-IP / X-Forwarded-For without checking the peer. Any client can (1) bypass the per-IP login bucket of T009 by sending a fresh X-Forwarded-For per attempt, (2) poison the fail2ban-facing failed-login records so a victim IP gets banned, and (3) satisfy a later 'peer is in DLTOOL_TRUSTED_PROXIES' check with a forged address, since the check would run on the already-rewritten RemoteAddr. Conversely nothing decides the cookie `Secure` flag or HSTS behind the shipped Caddy profile, where r.TLS is nil.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `internal/api/proxy.go` + `proxy_test.go` to T007's Files table: a CIDR-gated forwarded-header middleware built from `cfg.TrustedProxies` replacing `middleware.RealIP` (rewrite RemoteAddr and derive the scheme only when the direct peer matches a trusted prefix, taking the right-most untrusted XFF entry), exporting `api.ClientIP(r)` and `api.IsHTTPS(r)`, with a test that forged X-Forwarded-For/-Proto from an untrusted peer are ignored. Then: T009 step 7 keys the per-IP bucket and the failed-login record on `api.ClientIP`; T008/doc 05 section 1.2 set cookie `Secure` from `api.IsHTTPS`; T095 changes `SecurityHeaders` to `SecurityHeaders(cfg *config.Config) func(http.Handler) http.Handler` …

#### F143 · HIGH · Alpine's `7zip` package builds 7zz with DISABLE_RAR=1, so `.rar` extraction cannot work in the shipped image

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T074-auto-extract-archives.md:16`, `docs/tasks/T074-auto-extract-archives.md:140`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:67`, `docs/decisions/0011-alpine-runtime-with-puid-pgid.md:41`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:579`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T074: "A completed task whose payload is a `.zip`, `.tar`, `.gz`, `.tgz`, `.rar` or `.7z` archive is extracted by a `7zz` subprocess" and acceptance "One archive of each of `.zip`, `.tar`, `.gz`, `.tgz`, `.rar` and `.7z` extracts successfully." T124/ADR-0011: `RUN apk add --no-cache su-exec ca-certificates tzdata 7zip nodejs`. Live check of aports main/7zip/APKBUILD (fetched 2026-09-02): `license="LGPL-2.0-only"` and in build(): `# DISABLE_RAR: RAR codec is non-free` ... `make -f ../../cmpl_gcc.mak ... DISABLE_RAR=1`.
- **Impact:** Alpine strips the unRAR-licensed codec, so `/usr/bin/7zz` from `apk add 7zip` reports 'Can not open the file as archive' for every .rar. FR-100/T074's .rar criterion passes only on a developer host with an upstream 7zz and fails in the image; users get extract_failed_invalid_archive for every RAR release, which is the dominant scene format.
- **Suggested fix:** Decide explicitly and record it: (a) drop `.rar` from FR-100, T074's goal/acceptance and doc 12 section 4 for v1, or (b) add an ADR to ship upstream 7-Zip's static `7zz` (SHA-256-verified tarball, as ADR-0018 does for yt-dlp, noting the unRAR licence clause) and change T124's Dockerfile accordingly. Either way add a T124 acceptance check that matches the decision, e.g. `7zz i | grep -q Rar` must succeed (option b) or must fail (option a).

#### F151 · HIGH · Readiness wait `wait.ForHTTP("/api/v2/app/version")` targets an authenticated endpoint; qBittorrent answers 403 without a SID, so the default 200-only matcher never becomes ready

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:1207`, `docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:185`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:94`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:723`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** 06 §11: "`testcontainers.WithWaitStrategy(wait.ForHTTP(\"/api/v2/app/version\").WithPort(\"8080/tcp\"))`; the default wait deadline is 60 s." 13 §4 repeats it verbatim. T038 step 8: "starting `lscr.io/linuxserver/qbittorrent:5.2.3` through testcontainers with `wait.ForHTTP(\"/api/v2/app/version\").WithPort(\"8080/tcp\")`". 06 §5.2 itself states: "every method except `auth/login` requires the session."
- **Impact:** qBittorrent declares only `auth/login` public; an unauthenticated GET /api/v2/app/version from the Docker host (not localhost inside the container, so no bypass_local_auth) returns 403 Forbidden. testcontainers-go's ForHTTP default status matcher accepts only 200, so the strategy waits the full 60 s and fails; every qBittorrent integration test (T038, and ADR-0017's confirmation) errors before running.
- **Suggested fix:** Change all three homes (06 §11, 13 §4, T038 step 8) to `wait.ForHTTP("/api/v2/app/version").WithPort("8080/tcp").WithStatusCodeMatcher(func(s int) bool { return s == 200 || s == 403 })`, or wait on `wait.ForListeningPort("8080/tcp")` and then probe `auth/login`. Keep exactly one authoritative copy and have 13 §4 link to it.

#### F152 · HIGH · Create-time file selection never reaches qBittorrent: T033 fills AddRequest.SelectFiles/Extra, but T029's Add sends neither and no task performs the add-stopped → filePrio → start sequence that 03 §6.1 describes

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:149`, `docs/tasks/T033-uploads-subfolder-and-selection.md:113`, `docs/03-architecture.md:212`, `docs/02-requirements.md:121`, `docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:94`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T029 step 7: "Implement `Add` as `POST torrents/add` ...: `urls` for `req.URIs` newline-separated, one `torrents` part per `req.Blob` ..., `savepath`, `category`, comma-separated `tags`, `sequentialDownload`, and `autoTMM=false` explicitly." (no SelectFiles, no Extra priorities). T033 step 7: "Apply `select_files` to the first multi-file manifest of the submission: fill `AddRequest.SelectFiles`, and pass the priority map through `AddRequest.Extra` only when the routed engine declares `per_file_priority`". 03 §6.1: "ENG->>QBT: POST /api/v2/torrents/add with stopped true and paused true ... ENG->>QBT: POST /api/v2/torrents/filePrio, pipe-separated ids, priority 0 for unticked files ... ENG->>QBT: POST /api/v2/torrents/start". FR-007: "dl-tool shall accept a `files:[{index, selected, priority}]` list at creation and on an existing task". M2 exit: "A magnet added with a file-selection step …
- **Impact:** qBittorrent's torrents/add has no per-file priority parameter (the `filePriorities` row in 06 §5.3 is not a 5.2.3 add parameter as far as I can verify), so selection must be applied post-add. Neither T029 nor T033 nor T032 does it; a create-time selection silently downloads every file, FR-007's creation half fails and the M2 checkpoint is unreachable.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a step to T029 (or a new T032 step, since T033 depends on T032) specifying the qBittorrent create-time selection sequence: when `req.SelectFiles` or the priority map is set, add with `stopped=true`+`paused=true`, resolve the hash, wait for `has_metadata`, issue one `torrents/filePrio` per priority group, then `torrents/start`/`torrents/resume` unless `req.StartPaused`; name the exact `AddRequest.Extra` key T033 writes (e.g. `file_priorities` as index:priority pairs) in doc 06 §1 so both sides agree. Keep `filePriorities` in 06 §5.3 but mark it usable only for a `.torrent` blob whose manifest is already known.

#### F153 · HIGH · No document owns how the qBittorrent WebUI credentials are provisioned: compose passes an empty QBT_PASSWORD, 11 §8 makes that fatal, the linuxserver image has no password env, and the WebUI is unpublished

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:97`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:490`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:272`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:120`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:132`, `docs/tasks/T125-compose-and-env-example.md:16`, `docs/tasks/00-task-index.md:40`, `docs/decisions/0013-mandatory-built-in-authentication.md:32`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** 10 §2: "DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL: \"http://qbittorrent:8080\"\n DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_USERNAME: \"${QBT_USERNAME:-}\"\n DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_PASSWORD: \"${QBT_PASSWORD:-}\""; §6: "QBT_USERNAME=admin\nQBT_PASSWORD=". 11 §8: "| Variable unset | `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_USERNAME` or `..._PASSWORD` while `DLTOOL_QBITTORRENT_URL` is set | Fatal. | `config_missing` |". The qbittorrent service gets only `WEBUI_PORT` and `TORRENTING_PORT`; "# WebUI is deliberately NOT published: only dl-tool talks to it." ADR-0013 describes the engine's actual behaviour: "qBittorrent's post-4.6.1 model: a random temporary password printed to stdout at each start." T125 Goal: "`cp .env.example .env && docker compose ... up -d --build` starts `dl-tool` and `qbittorrent` ... and `curl -s localhost:8091/healthz` answers". 10 §8.2 additionally requires "qBittorrent's 'Bypass authentication for clients on localhost' must be …
- **Impact:** Following the plan literally: the M0 checkpoint stack either exits with `config_missing` (password empty while the URL is hard-coded) or boots with a BitTorrent lane that can never log in, because the linuxserver image takes no password env, prints a random temporary password to its log, and its WebUI (the only supported place to set a permanent password) is never published. No runbook entry, .env comment, T125 step or T101 action tells the operator to set `WebUI\Password_PBKDF2` in qBittorrent.conf or to call app/setPreferences via docker exec. The M7 'quickstart brings the stack up from scratch' claim is unreachable.
- **Suggested fix:** Own the credential procedure in doc 10 (a new §2 note plus a doc 17 §8 troubleshooting row) and reference it from T125 and the README quickstart: either (a) document a one-shot `docker compose logs qbittorrent | grep 'temporary password'` -> `docker compose exec` call to `app/setPreferences` setting `web_ui_username`/`web_ui_password`, or (b) mount a seeded `qBittorrent.conf` containing `WebUI\\Username` and `WebUI\\Password_PBKDF2` derived from `QBT_PASSWORD`. Also document how `bypass_local_auth` is enabled for the doc 10 §8.2 port-forward command. Do not duplicate F028's compose fix here; a single treatment of the URL/secret pair covers both engines.

#### F167 · HIGH · Runtime image ships no ffmpeg, so yt-dlp cannot merge or remux and silently caps media downloads at pre-merged formats

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:406`, `docs/tasks/T124-runtime-dockerfile-and-entrypoint.md:67`, `docs/tasks/T093-harden-runtime-image.md:108`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:1035`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 10 line 406 / T124 line 67: "RUN apk add --no-cache su-exec ca-certificates tzdata 7zip nodejs". T093 step 7: "Leave every line T124 wrote that this contract does not name — the `apk add` set ... byte for byte unchanged." doc 06 line 1035 discusses only the JS runtime as "required for full YouTube support". A grep of the whole plan for ffmpeg/ffprobe returns nothing.
- **Impact:** yt-dlp's README lists ffmpeg/ffprobe as required for merging separate video and audio streams and for most post-processing. Without ffmpeg yt-dlp's default format selector degrades to best/bestvideo+bestaudio (single pre-merged file), which on YouTube means at most 720p progressive; DASH-only and many HLS sources fail outright, and any postprocess: progress or remux step never exists. The media lane therefore under-delivers Download Station parity while every task's acceptance criteria still pass, because nothing in T087-T113 exercises a merged format.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `ffmpeg` to the runtime `apk add` line in doc 10 §5 and T124 (and name it in T093 step 7 so the frozen set includes it), record the new runtime dependency in an ADR line per AGENTS rule 3 (it is a ~70 MB image cost), extend T113's boot capability probe to run `ffmpeg -version` and surface it in `GET /system/info` beside the yt-dlp and JS-runtime versions, and add a T090 contract subtest that completes a `bv*+ba` fixture. If ffmpeg is deliberately excluded, doc 06 §7 must state the pre-merged-format ceiling and the argv must pass an explicit `-f b` so the fallback is intentional.
