- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T091: `func (s *Store) BackupInto(ctx context.Context, dir string) (BackupResult, error)` and four more `func (s *Store) Prune…` methods. T092: `func (s *Store) GetSettings(ctx context.Context) (Settings, error)`. Its dependency T006 creates only `func Open(ctx context.Context, dbPath, backupDir string) (*sqlx.DB, error)`; T017 creates a differently named type: `type TaskStore struct{ db *sqlx.DB }` / `func NewTaskStore(db *sqlx.DB) *TaskStore`. `grep -rn 'type Store struct' docs/` returns nothing anywhere in the plan. T091 step 6 then says `Create internal/api/system.go with SystemHandlers, its constructor taking the store and the config directory`, while the only server constructor is `func NewServer(cfg *config.Config, db *sqlx.DB, log *slog.Logger) (*Server, error)` — it receives a `*sqlx.DB`, never a store.
- **Impact:** The two M6/M7 tasks in this batch cannot compile: the receiver type, its fields and its constructor do not exist and neither Files table contains a file where they could be declared without colliding with whatever T027 (which first uses `*Store` in internal/store/settings.go) invented. The api layer additionally has no defined way to obtain a store value, so `SystemHandlers` cannot be constructed inside server.go.
- **Suggested fix:** Adopt the finding's fix: declare `type Store struct{ db *sqlx.DB }` and `func NewStore(db *sqlx.DB) *Store` in T006's interface contract (it already owns `internal/store/db.go`), state there that every later `internal/store/*.go` file hangs its queries on that receiver, and reconcile T017's `TaskStore` (either fold it in or say explicitly that it is a separate type). Then change T007's `NewServer` signature to take a `*store.Store` (or to call `store.NewStore(db)`) so the `SettingsHandlers`/`SystemHandlers` constructors named in T027, T091 and T092 have a value to receive.

#### F647 · HIGH · `go install github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/cmd/golangci-lint@$(GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION)` is the v1-only module path, so `make setup` fails for any current pin

- **Where:** `docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:56`, `docs/tasks/T001-makefile-and-doclint.md:47`, `docs/tasks/T001-makefile-and-doclint.md:97`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** Doc 13 §2 setup: "$(GO) install github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/cmd/golangci-lint@$(GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION)" with "GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION ?= PINME # pin at implementation time; make setup fails until it is set". T001 acceptance: "`GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION` and `LYCHEE_VERSION` both hold a real version string, not `PINME`."
- **Impact:** golangci-lint v2 (the current major since 2025) is module `github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2`; its binary package is `github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2/cmd/golangci-lint`. An agent pinning any v2.x version gets "module ... found, but does not contain package .../cmd/golangci-lint" and `make setup` fails, which fails every CI job (T002 runs `make setup` first in each job). The task gives no guidance to pin the abandoned v1 line instead.
- **Suggested fix:** Change docs/13-testing-and-verification.md:56 to `$(GO) install github.com/golangci/golangci-lint/v2/cmd/golangci-lint@$(GOLANGCI_LINT_VERSION)` and change the comment at :48 to state that the pin must be a v2.x tag (v1.64.8 is the final v1 release, predates Go 1.26 and cannot type-check a Go 1.26 module). T001 copies the head verbatim, so no separate T001 edit is needed beyond re-reading doc 13 §2. No `.golangci.yml` exists anywhere in the plan (T123:169 forbids one), so v2's `version: "2"` config requirement does not apply — defaults are used.


### M1 — task core and the aria2 engine

#### F002 · CRITICAL · DELETE /tasks/{id} hard-deletes the tasks row (cascading task_events away) while doc 04 defines `removed` as a retained soft-delete and T022's `remove` action transitions to `removed`

- **Where:** `docs/05-api-contract.md:503`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:504`, `docs/tasks/T023-remove-task-and-data.md:32`, `docs/tasks/T023-remove-task-and-data.md:73`, `docs/tasks/T023-remove-task-and-data.md:93`, `docs/tasks/T023-remove-task-and-data.md:106`, `docs/04-data-model.md:305`, `docs/04-data-model.md:535`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 05 §5.6 step 5: `The row survives the task row through the cascade window and is written before the response`; step 6: `Delete the row | `tasks`, and its `task_files`, `task_trackers` and `task_tags` rows by cascade`. T023: `Add `Delete` and `ListFiles``, `Implement steps 5 and 6 in one transaction`, acceptance `A successful `delete_data=true` writes exactly one `task.data_deleted` event at level `warn``. doc 04: `task_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES tasks(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (task_events) and `| `removed` | Soft-deleted; the row is retained for the event log. |`. T022: `| `remove` | `Engine.Remove` | any → `removed` |`.
- **Impact:** SQLite cascades immediately: deleting the `tasks` row inside the same transaction deletes the `task.data_deleted` event written one statement earlier, so T023's acceptance criterion cannot be observed and the audit record of the only irreversible operation is lost; `GET /tasks/{id}/events` for the deleted task returns 404. Meanwhile `POST /tasks/actions {"action":"remove"}` leaves a `removed` row, so the two removal paths produce different database states and the `all` filter (`every state except removed`) only makes sense for one of them. Two authoritative documents disagree on a fact both T022 and T023 must implement.
- **Suggested fix:** Make 05 s5.6 step 6 a soft delete: `set state='removed', engine_ref=NULL`, no row deletion and no cascade; T023's store method becomes `MarkRemoved` (not `Delete`) and only T091/T074's retention sweep ever hard-deletes. Delete the phrase 'survives the task row through the cascade window' from 05:503 and add task_events to any cascade discussion. If hard delete is kept instead, doc 04 s4.1 must drop `removed`, doc 03 s8.1's `any -> removed` row must go, and task_events.task_id must become `ON DELETE SET NULL` - but then T022's `remove` action needs rewriting too.

#### F003 · CRITICAL · Admission control is bypassed: T020 hands every new task to the engine at creation and T022 resumes directly, while T098 expects to be the only releaser

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:122`, `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:123`, `docs/tasks/T022-task-actions-and-patch.md:93`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:107`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:108`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:73`, `docs/03-architecture.md:285`, `docs/03-architecture.md:305`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T020 step 8: "insert the `tasks` row through `store.Create` in state `queued` (or `paused` when `paused` is true), then call `Engine.Add` and store the returned handle with `SetEngineRef`." T022 resume row: "| `resume` | `Engine.Resume` | `paused` -> `queued`, released by T098's admission pass |". T098 step 5: "Release a candidate by calling `Engine.Add` when `EngineRef` is nil, or `Engine.Resume` when it is set, then transition to `downloading`". T026: "dl-tool holds a task with `engine_ref` still `NULL` | Leave it in `queued`; T098's admission pass owns starting it." doc 03 s6.4: "dl-tool is therefore the only admission controller: it holds tasks in `queued` and releases them itself" and the sequence "TICK->>DB: select queued candidates in process_order ... ENG->>E: add or resume". doc 03 s6.1 instead shows POST /tasks doing "POST /api/v2/torrents/add ... POST /api/v2/torrents/start …
- **Impact:** With T020 as written every created task is already transferring in its engine before T098 runs, so max_active_* (FR-020/FR-021/FR-123) is never enforced for new tasks and T026 never sees a queued task with a NULL engine_ref. T098's SelectQueuedCandidates then finds queued rows that already hold an engine_ref and calls Engine.Resume on downloads that are not paused (aria2.unpause on an active GID is an RPC error), and T022 calls Engine.Resume itself while also marking the task queued for a second Resume by T098. Doc 03 contradicts itself between s6.1 and s6.4. T098's Files table does not touch internal/api/tasks.go, so nothing later repairs T020.
- **Suggested fix:** Make T098 the sole caller of Engine.Add/Engine.Resume: change T020 step 8 to insert with `engine_ref` NULL and never call `Engine.Add` (creation stays `queued`, or `paused` when requested), change T022's resume row to a pure `paused -> queued` transition with no engine call, and rewrite doc 03 §6.1's sequence so the add/start happens in the admission pass. If instead T020 keeps the call, it must pass StartPaused=true and leave the row `queued`, and doc 03 §6.4 plus T098 step 5 must say so.

#### F004 · CRITICAL · No legal transition reaches `completed` for a plain download, and post-processing entry state contradicts between doc 03/T017 (from `downloading`) and T074/T076 (from `completed`)

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:117`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:118`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:121`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:123`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:149`, `docs/03-architecture.md:388`, `docs/03-architecture.md:389`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:634`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T017 legal table: "| `queued` | `downloading` |", "| `downloading` | `checking`, `seeding`, `extracting`, `moving` |", "| `seeding` | `completed` |", "| `moving` | `completed`, `seeding` |" and acceptance "Every pair in the transition table succeeds and every other pair returns `ErrIllegalTransition`." doc 03 s8.1: "| `downloading` | `extracting` / `moving` / `seeding` | Payload complete: extract if enabled, else move if the destination differs, else seed. |". doc 06 s4.6 maps aria2 "| `complete` | `completed` |" and s1 says "No engine ever reports `extracting` or `moving`... Adapters return only the other eight." T074: "Chain is the post-processing pipeline. It is enqueued once per task that reaches completed" and "SetExtractProgress writes tasks.unzip_progress (0-100) and holds state at 'extracting'." T076: "Handle transitions the task to moving, calls fsx.Move, writes …
- **Impact:** An HTTP/FTP download with no extraction and no move (the M1 exit checkpoint) can never become `completed`: aria2 reports `complete`, the reconciler calls Transition(downloading -> completed), and T017 returns ErrIllegalTransition on every tick. Later, T074/T076 run the chain from `completed` (completed -> extracting/moving -> completed), which T017 also forbids (no completed->extracting, completed->moving or extracting->completed rows), so auto-extract and cross-device moves fail at the store as well. The plan holds two incompatible models of when post-processing starts.
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one model and write it into T017's table, 03-architecture.md s8.1 and T074/T076 together. Recommended: engines drive `downloading -> completed` (and `checking -> completed`, `queued -> completed` for a file that finishes between polls) and `downloading -> seeding`; the post-processing chain then runs from `completed`, so add `completed -> extracting`, `completed -> moving`, `extracting -> moving`, `extracting -> completed`, `moving -> completed`; keep `seeding -> completed` for the share-limit end and drop `moving -> seeding`. Update 03 s8.1's rows to match and add every new pair to T017's table test (T017:141).

#### F010 · CRITICAL · T026's vanished-handle rule contradicts doc 17 §1.6 (its own cited context) and NFR-003, and is not scoped to non-terminal tasks

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:72`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:85`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:25`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:11`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:79`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:147`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:148`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:149`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T026:72 '| dl-tool holds a task whose handle the engine no longer knows | Transition to `error` with `error_code` `engine_unavailable` and one `engine.unavailable` event. |' and T026:85 'Mark a task whose handle has vanished as `error`'. T026:25 lists doc 17 as context #4 and T026:11 claims 'Implements NFR-003 (resume every task after a restart)'. doc 17:148 '| no | `downloading`, `seeding`, `checking` | Re-submit from the stored source with resume semantics (`--continue` for aria2 and yt-dlp; re-add by infohash for qBittorrent), then adopt the new `engine_ref`. |'; doc 17:149 '| no | `queued`, `paused` | Leave as is.'; doc 17:147 'Emit `task.reconciled`'; doc 17:154 'Implement the "no handle" row as the expected path for aria2.' doc 17 scopes the sweep to 'every task not in a terminal state (`completed`, `removed`, `error`)'; T026:79 joins Engine.List against every engine_ref with no …
- **Impact:** Because aria2 GIDs do not survive a daemon restart (doc 17:154), T026 as written marks every in-flight aria2 task `error` on every restart instead of re-submitting it — the opposite of NFR-003. Without a non-terminal filter, every `removed` (bulk action) or force-completed task, whose GID was removed via aria2.remove/removeDownloadResult, is flipped to `error` on the next 1 s poll ('any → error' is universal), so no task can stay `completed` or `removed`.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite T026's rules table and steps to match 17-operations s1.6 verbatim: restrict the sweep to non-terminal states (add a state filter to `ListByEngineRef`, or return state with the id); for a vanished handle in downloading/seeding/checking re-submit from `source_uri`/infohash with resume semantics and `SetEngineRef` to the new handle, emitting `task.reconciled`; leave queued/paused untouched; reserve `error`+`engine_unavailable` for a re-submission that itself fails. Then align NFR-003 (02-requirements.md:981) with that wording and replace `TestVanishedHandleErrors` with a re-submission test plus a completed/removed no-op test.

#### F023 · CRITICAL · qBittorrent ownership predicate is never installed: T030 defaults to 'accept nothing' and no task calls SetOwnershipFilter, so every qBittorrent task is invisible and the reconciler marks it engine_unavailable

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T030-qbittorrent-maindata-deltas.md:63`, `docs/tasks/T030-qbittorrent-maindata-deltas.md:97`, `docs/tasks/T030-qbittorrent-maindata-deltas.md:27`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:72`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:29`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:166`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T030: "// SetOwnershipFilter installs the predicate deciding whether a qBittorrent hash belongs to a dl-tool\n// task; it is supplied by the Reconciler of T026. ... Until it is set, the cache holds nothing." and step 4: "default the predicate to one that accepts nothing, so a caller that forgets to install it sees an empty queue rather than foreign transfers." T030's Files table lists only internal/engine/qbittorrent/* files. T026 (M1, finished before T029/T030 exist) has no step that calls SetOwnershipFilter and its Files table is internal/engine/reconcile.go, reconcile_test.go and internal/engine/aria2/client.go. T026 rule: "dl-tool holds a task whose handle the engine no longer knows | Transition to `error` with `error_code` `engine_unavailable`". T029 step 12 only says "build the client ... and `Register` it beside aria2". T038/T100/T101 Files tables contain no reconcile.go or …
- **Impact:** With the predicate never set, Client.List/Get/Events return nothing for every torrent. The reconciler then sees every qBittorrent task's handle as 'no longer known' and transitions all of them to error/engine_unavailable on the first sweep; SSE never carries a qBittorrent delta. The M2 exit checkpoint ('a magnet ... downloads and seeds') is unreachable and FR-148's qBittorrent half is asserted by a test that only proves the adapter hides everything.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a step and a file to T030 that installs the predicate: either give the Reconciler an exported hook (`Reconciler.OwnedRefs(engine string) func(string) bool`) and add `internal/engine/reconcile.go` to T030's Files table, or drop the adapter-side filter entirely and let T026 do the foreign-transfer join for qBittorrent exactly as it does for aria2 (T019:127-131 already sets that pattern). Whichever is chosen, the T030 comment must name the task that actually supplies the predicate, and sync_test.go's TestForeignHashIsInvisible must exercise the installed path, not the default-deny one.

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:37`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:166`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:196`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:176`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:82`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:33`

- **Evidence:** T029 Files: "| `internal/api/server.go` | modify | Construct the client from config and register it in the engine registry. |"; step 12: "build the client from `cfg.QBittorrentURL` ... and `Register` it beside aria2"; Out of scope: "Do NOT implement `List`, `Get` or `Events`; T030 owns ... Leave them absent until then — do not add a stub that returns nil."; acceptance: "`Registry.Get(\"qbittorrent\")` returns the client after `NewServer` with a configured URL." T019: "func (r *Registry) Register(e Engine)". T038 Files: "| `internal/engine/qbittorrent/client.go` | modify | Add the `var _ engine.Engine = (*Client)(nil)` assertion. |"
- **Impact:** Go interface satisfaction is static: passing *qbittorrent.Client to Register(e Engine) fails to compile until every method of docs/06 §1 exists, which is only after T038. `make lint`/`make test` for T029 (and every task in between) cannot pass without either adding forbidden stubs or breaking the Files-table rule. T029's last acceptance criterion is unsatisfiable as written.
- **Suggested fix:** Move the `internal/api/server.go` registration and the `Registry.Get("qbittorrent")` acceptance criterion out of T029 into T038 (where the interface assertion lands), removing server.go from T029's Files table; or, if registration must happen early, state explicitly in T029 that the missing methods are implemented as `return engine.ErrNotSupported` placeholders owned by the later tasks and relax the 'do not add a stub' rule for them. Note the same hole affects the intermediate tasks T030/T032/T036/T037, whose builds also include server.go.

#### F096 · CRITICAL · Transition table forbids the transitions the engine normalisation tables produce (downloading→completed, paused→seeding/completed, queued→seeding, checking→seeding, completed→seeding)

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:117`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:120`, `docs/03-architecture.md:389`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:634`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:832`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:833`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:835`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:53`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T017 legal table: `queued`→`downloading`; `downloading`→`checking, seeding, extracting, moving`; `checking`→`downloading`; `paused`→`downloading, queued`; `seeding`→`completed`; `extracting`→`moving`; `moving`→`completed, seeding`; `error`→`queued` (plus any→paused/error/removed), and "It returns ErrIllegalTransition and mutates nothing when the move is not permitted." Doc 06 §4.6 aria2: `complete` → `completed` (an HTTP download goes straight from `downloading` to `completed`). Doc 06 §5.6 qBittorrent: `uploading, forcedUP, stalledUP` → `seeding`; `queuedDL, queuedUP` → `queued`; `pausedUP or stoppedUP` → `completed` when `progress == 1`, else `paused`; `checkingUP` → `checking`. T026 and T098 drive state through the same `Transition(ctx, id, next, code, message)`.
- **Impact:** The poller/reconciler (T026) cannot persist ordinary engine outcomes: an aria2 HTTP download finishing (`downloading`→`completed`), a seeding torrent that the user paused then resumed (`paused`→`seeding`, or `paused`→`completed` when qBittorrent reports `stoppedUP`), a completed torrent re-entering seeding (`completed`→`seeding`), a finished torrent leaving qBittorrent's upload queue (`queued`→`seeding`) or a recheck of finished data (`checking`→`seeding`/`completed`). Each returns ErrIllegalTransition on every poll tick, so tasks freeze in a stale state and M1/M2 exit checkpoints ("progress streams… pause/resume/remove work", "downloads and seeds") are unreachable.
- **Suggested fix:** Extend 03 §8.1 and the T017 table with the engine-driven edges — `downloading`->`completed`; `queued`->`checking, seeding, completed`; `checking`->`seeding, completed`; `paused`->`seeding, checking, completed`; `completed`->`seeding, checking, moving`; `seeding`->`downloading, checking` — and state explicitly that the reconciler may adopt any engine-reported non-terminal state, keeping `removed` terminal. Add a T017 test that every target state of the 06 §4.6 and §5.6 tables is reachable from every state those tables can precede.

#### F034 · HIGH · Client liveness rule (offline after 2 s of silence, tracked from heartbeat comments) contradicts the server contract (push only on change, comment every 15 s) and browser EventSource never surfaces comment lines

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T051-event-stream-and-reconnect.md:82`, `docs/tasks/T051-event-stream-and-reconnect.md:96`, `docs/09-web-ui-spec.md:855`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:653`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:690`, `docs/tasks/T025-rid-deltas-over-sse.md:92`, `docs/tasks/T025-rid-deltas-over-sse.md:108`, `docs/09-web-ui-spec.md:860`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T051: `| no message for more than 2 s | `connection = 'offline'`, dot amber, grid dimmed to 70 %. |` and step 3 `Track liveness from the payloads and the heartbeat comment: more than 2 s without either means offline.` doc 09 §10.8: `On the first missed heartbeat, more than twice the 1 s tick, the connection dot turns amber`. doc 05 §6.1: `The server pushes at most once per second, and only when something changed.` / `Emit a comment line every 15 seconds while idle`. T025: `Heartbeat | one SSE comment every 15 seconds while idle`. Also doc 09: `fall back to polling ... every 3 s` vs doc 05: `every 2 seconds` (T051 notes this one).
- **Impact:** On any idle queue the server is silent for up to 15 s, so the SPA flips to `offline` and dims the grid after 2 s, then flips back on the next delta, on every idle period; the 5 s banner and possibly the 3-failure polling ladder trigger on a healthy connection. Worse, the WHATWG EventSource interface discards comment lines entirely, so the client can never observe `: hb` — the heartbeat only serves proxies, which doc 05 correctly says, but doc 09 and T051 build the liveness detector on it. This is adjacent to the assigned files (T051 is not assigned) but the contradiction is with doc 05 §6.1 and T025.
- **Suggested fix:** Settle liveness in doc 05 §6.1 (its single home): either the server emits an observable keep-alive — a named `event: ping` with empty data, or a `sync` carrying the unchanged rid — every N seconds and the client's offline threshold becomes > 2N, or the client derives liveness only from `EventSource.readyState`/`onerror` and the 2 s rule is deleted. Then rewrite doc 09 §10.8 rule 1 (drop `the 1 s tick`) and T051:82/96 to match, and align doc 09 §10.8 rule 4 with the 2 s of doc 05 §6.1 so T051:53-55 no longer has to document a discrepancy.

#### F035 · HIGH · Engine state mappings produce transitions T017 rejects (checking from seeding/completed/queued and back, paused->seeding, downloading->queued) and T026 never says what the reconciler does on ErrIllegalTransition

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:833`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:836`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:832`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:632`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:118`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:119`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:120`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:149`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06 s5.6: "| `checkingDL`, `checkingUP`, `checkingResumeData`, `moving` | `checking` |", "| `queuedDL`, `queuedUP`, `allocating` | `queued` |", "| `uploading`, `forcedUP`, `stalledUP` | `seeding` |"; s4.6 "| `waiting` | `queued` |". T017 allows only "| `downloading` | `checking`, `seeding`, `extracting`, `moving` |", "| `checking` | `downloading` |", "| `paused` | `downloading`, `queued` |" and asserts "every other pair returns `ErrIllegalTransition`". T022: "| `recheck` | ... | `downloading` -> `checking` when supported |" and "| `force_complete` | `Engine.Remove` with `deleteData` false | any -> `completed` |"; T023 step 10 "Implement `force_complete=true` as a transition to `completed`". T026 step 4: "call `Transition` only when the normalised state differs from the stored one". T098 step 5 transitions to `downloading` before the engine reports anything.
- **Impact:** Real engine sequences hit illegal pairs every day: qBittorrent puts every torrent through `checkingResumeData` at daemon start (completed/seeding/queued -> checking), a recheck of a finished torrent is `checkingUP` (seeding/completed -> checking -> seeding/completed), a resumed finished torrent goes paused -> seeding, `queuedDL` or aria2 `waiting` after T098 has already written `downloading` is downloading -> queued. `force_complete` (any -> completed) and `recheck` in T022/T023 contradict T017 outright. T026 does not specify whether the reconciler drops, logs, forces or retries on ErrIllegalTransition, so the poll loop's behaviour on these paths is undefined and tasks can wedge in a stale …
- **Suggested fix:** Extend the T017 table and 03 §8.1 with every pair the doc 06 normalisation tables can emit — seeding->checking, completed->checking, queued->checking, checking->seeding, checking->completed, checking->queued, paused->seeding, completed->seeding, downloading->queued — and add `any -> completed` for force_complete (or make force_complete route through seeding/moving). Add one sentence to T026 stating the reconciler's behaviour when Transition returns ErrIllegalTransition (log once at warn with the from/to pair, leave the row, write no event) and one acceptance criterion covering it.

#### F036 · HIGH · `stoppedUP` with progress 1 maps to `completed`, so a user-paused seeding torrent can never be `paused`; doc 03's `seeding -> paused` edge is unreachable and the reconciler then attempts paused -> completed, which T017 forbids

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:835`, `docs/03-architecture.md:365`, `docs/03-architecture.md:390`, `docs/04-data-model.md:529`, `docs/tasks/T022-task-actions-and-patch.md:92`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:120`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:88`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06 s5.6: "| `pausedUP` **or** `stoppedUP` | `completed` when `progress == 1`, else `paused` |". doc 03 diagram "seeding --> paused" and table "| any | `paused` | User pause, or the bandwidth schedule paused the task. |". doc 04: "| `paused` | Paused by a user. |". T022: "| `pause` | `Engine.Pause` | any -> `paused` |". T017: "| `paused` | `downloading`, `queued` |".
- **Impact:** qBittorrent reports the same state (`stoppedUP`, progress 1) for 'share limit reached, seeding over' and for 'user stopped a seeding torrent'. The mapping folds both into `completed`, so after a user pauses a seeding torrent (T022 writes `paused`) the next poll normalises to `completed` and Transition(paused -> completed) fails; if it were allowed the user's pause would be silently overwritten and the schedule's No-Download parking (T081, which looks for the latest `task.schedule.paused` event on a `paused` row) would lose its parked set. `seeding -> paused` in doc 03 is unreachable for the only seeding engine.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the mapping stateful in doc 06 §5.6 and reference it from T029 and T030: `stoppedUP`/`pausedUP` with progress 1 maps to `paused` when the stored state is `paused` or dl-tool issued the stop, and to `completed` only otherwise (share limit reached). Add `paused -> seeding` and `completed -> seeding` to T017's transition table so a resumed seed is representable, and add a T029 mapping-table test row for the user-stopped seed.

#### F037 · HIGH · Engine handle shape is inconsistent: T019's Add returns a bare GID, T029's Add returns a namespaced id, T020 stores whichever comes back in engine_ref, and T026 joins engine_ref against namespaced List ids

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:36`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:38`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:100`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:144`, `docs/tasks/T018-aria2-status-mapping.md:118`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:158`, `docs/tasks/T030-qbittorrent-maindata-deltas.md:100`, `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:123`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06 s1: "Engine task ids are **namespaced by engine** ... `"aria2:2089b05ecca3d829"` ... The store splits them into the `engine` and `engine_ref` columns". T019: "| `Add` with URIs | `aria2.addUri([secret], uris, options)` -- returns one GID string |" and acceptance "`Add` with two URIs sends one `aria2.addUri` call and returns the GID string." T018 step 5 fills "`ID` as `"aria2:" + gid`". T029 step 9: "Return `"qbittorrent:" + hash`." T030: "`Get` splits the `"qbittorrent:"` prefix". T020 step 8: "store the returned handle with `SetEngineRef`". T026: "ListByEngineRef(...) (map[string]string, error) // engine_ref -> task id" and step 2 "joined against `ListByEngineRef` on the `engine_ref` value". doc 03 s8.8: "a transfer is foreign when its `engine_ref` matches no `tasks` row".
- **Impact:** Following the tasks literally, aria2 rows get engine_ref = `<gid>` while qBittorrent rows get engine_ref = `qbittorrent:<hash>`; the unique index and the foreign-task join then compare bare GIDs against `aria2:<gid>` List ids, so every aria2 task is 'vanished' (marked error) and every listed handle is 'foreign'. Nothing states whether Get/Pause/Remove take the namespaced or bare form, and T017's SetEngineRef does not split.
- **Suggested fix:** State one convention in doc 06 §1 and repeat it in T016's contract: every id crossing the Engine interface — the `Add` result, `TaskInfo.ID`, and the id argument of Get/Files/Pause/Resume/Remove/SetFiles/SetLocation/Rename/SetCategory/SetRateLimits/SetShareLimits — is namespaced `<engine>:<handle>`; the store strips the prefix in `SetEngineRef` and the reconciler strips it before joining `ListByEngineRef`. Change T019's mapping row and acceptance line to `returns "aria2:" + gid`, and say explicitly in T017 that SetEngineRef stores the bare handle.

#### F038 · HIGH · Bare-infohash submissions cannot reach the router: T015's Normalize rejects any string without a supported scheme, T016 requires bare hashes to route via Normalize, and doc 05 vs doc 06/T016 disagree on which bare forms are accepted

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T015-normalise-submitted-uris.md:110`, `docs/tasks/T015-normalise-submitted-uris.md:113`, `docs/tasks/T016-engine-interface-and-router.md:75`, `docs/tasks/T016-engine-interface-and-router.md:95`, `docs/tasks/T016-engine-interface-and-router.md:102`, `docs/tasks/T016-engine-interface-and-router.md:110`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:267`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:361`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T015 step 5: classify "`magnet:` and a path ending `.torrent` ... `.metalink`/`.meta4` ... `http`/`https` ... `ftp`/`ftps` ... `sftp` -- and return `ErrUnsupportedScheme` for anything else." T016 row 2: "`KindMagnet`, `KindTorrent`, or a bare 40-hex or 64-hex infohash | `NameQBittorrent`", step 5 "Detect a bare infohash in `Route` by length and alphabet only", step 7 "Drive every row through `uri.Normalize` first", acceptance "`Route` returns `NameQBittorrent` for a magnet, a `.torrent` URL and a bare 40-hex infohash." doc 06 s2 row 2: "a bare 40-hex or 64-hex infohash". doc 05 s5.2 `uris`: "bare 40-hex or 32-base32 infohash". T100 step 4: "from a bare 40-hex or 64-hex submission".
- **Impact:** A bare hash has no `://`, so Normalize returns ErrUnsupportedScheme and T016's router test row and acceptance criterion cannot pass as written; T020 would reject bare hashes with /problems/unsupported-scheme. `Kind` has no value for a bare hash (source_kind enum has none), and the accepted alphabet differs: doc 05 promises base32 (32 chars), doc 06/T016/T100 promise 64-hex, neither promises both.
- **Suggested fix:** In T015 have `Normalize` recognise a bare 40-hex, 32-base32 and 64-hex string and rewrite it to a canonical magnet (`magnet:?xt=urn:btih:<40hex>` / `urn:btmh:1220<64hex>`) with `Kind = KindMagnet` and InfohashV1/V2 filled; then T016 row 2 needs only KindMagnet/KindTorrent and step 5 can be deleted. Align 05:361 and 06:267 on the same three accepted forms.

Transition" yet the rule reads "Transition to `error` with `error_code` `engine_unavailable` and one `engine.unavailable` event" and acceptance "A vanished handle leaves its task in `error` with `error_code` `engine_unavailable`." T017's Transition is "Transition(ctx, id, next, code, message string)" where code is the task_events code, and T017 declares only Create/Get/UpdateProgress/SetEngineRef/Transition/AppendEvent. T074 (M6): "func (s *TaskStore) SetErrorCode(ctx context.Context, taskID, code, message string) error". T100 step 8 also needs "pause the task with …

#### F040 · HIGH · The reconciler can only persist state and six counters; name, content_path, error_code/message, infohashes, peers, ratio and completed_at from TaskInfo never reach the tasks table

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:70`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:83`, `docs/tasks/T017-task-store-and-state-machine.md:89`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:130`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:131`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:133`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:135`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:139`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T026 rule: "Engine reports a handle dl-tool knows | Write the normalised `TaskInfo` back: state, byte counters, rates, ETA." T017 Progress: "TotalBytes *int64; CompletedBytes int64; UploadedBytes int64; DownloadRate int64; UploadRate int64; ETASeconds *int64". doc 06 TaskInfo carries "ContentPath string // absolute path to the finished file or directory", "ErrorCode string // a tasks.error_code value", "InfohashV1 string", "NumSeeds *int", "CompletedAt *time.Time". doc 05 Task object exposes error_code, error_message, content_path, ratio, total_peers, connected_seeders, connected_leechers, started_at, completed_at.
- **Impact:** T018's whole errorCode table and aria2 error messages are computed and discarded; a magnet's real name after metadata, content_path for aria2/yt-dlp tasks, peer counts and ratio never update; completed_at/started_at are never set. The API and grid show stale or null values, and T100's back-fill of infohashes has to be bolted onto the adapter (see the layering finding) because the reconciler has no field for them.
- **Suggested fix:** Widen `store.Progress` (or add `TaskStore.ApplyEngineInfo`) with Name, ContentPath, InfohashV1/V2, NumSeeds, NumPeers, Ratio, StartedAt and CompletedAt, and change T026's rule row to 'write back every TaskInfo field the row stores', keeping error_code/error_message on the existing `Transition` path. Have the reconciler, not the qBittorrent sync adapter, call `SetInfohashes` from TaskInfo.InfohashV1/V2 (T100 step 7).

#### F041 · HIGH · `removed` is defined as a retained soft-delete, but DELETE /tasks and the bulk delete executor hard-delete the row (cascading task_events away)

- **Where:** `docs/04-data-model.md:535`, `docs/04-data-model.md:712`, `docs/04-data-model.md:622`, `docs/tasks/T023-remove-task-and-data.md:73`, `docs/tasks/T111-delete-data-and-hardlink-safety.md:88`, `docs/tasks/T022-task-actions-and-patch.md:94`, `docs/tasks/T100-infohash-identity-and-duplicates.md:85`, `docs/tasks/T021-list-and-filter-tasks.md:71`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 04 s4.1: "| `removed` | Soft-deleted; the row is retained for the event log. | none |"; s7: "`rule_matches`, `rule_seen_episodes`, `tasks` and every configuration table are never pruned automatically."; s4.7 quota query "WHERE owner_id = :user AND state <> 'removed'". T023 step 6: "| 6 | Delete the row | `tasks`; `task_files`, `task_trackers` and `task_tags` go by cascade. |". T111: "the row goes and every byte stays." T022: "| `remove` | `Engine.Remove` | any -> `removed` |". T100 FindByInfohash filters "`state != 'removed'`". task_events has "task_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES tasks(id) ON DELETE CASCADE".
- **Impact:** Two authoritative descriptions of what deletion does. If rows are hard-deleted the `removed` state, the `state <> 'removed'` filters in doc 04/T021/T100 and the 'retained for the event log' promise are dead, and the history the retention policy protects is cascade-deleted. If rows are soft-deleted, T023/T111 are wrong and the SSE `tasks_removed` derivation in T025 (ids absent from the next snapshot) needs the snapshot to exclude `removed` rows, which T025 does not say.
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one and propagate it. Soft-delete: T023 step 6 and T111 step 6 become `Transition(removed)` + clear `engine_ref`, T025's snapshot must exclude `state = 'removed'` rows (say so in T025 step 1), and the partial unique indices on infohash must be made conditional on state so a re-add is possible. Hard-delete: drop the `removed` row from doc 04 §4.1, remove `tasks` from doc 04 §7's never-pruned list, delete the `state <> 'removed'` predicates in doc 04 §4.7, T021 and T100, fix T022's transition row, and keep `removed` only as a transient SSE/`tasks_removed` marker.

#### F042 · HIGH · T100 makes the qBittorrent adapter call the store (SetInfohashes) from sync.go, violating the layering rule and T019's explicit prohibition, with no injection point in T100's Files table

- **Kind:** scope · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T100 Files: "| `internal/engine/qbittorrent/sync.go` | modify | Write both hashes back when metadata resolves. |"; step 7: "Edit `internal/engine/qbittorrent/sync.go` so the delta path calls back into `SetInfohashes`". doc 03 s5.2: "Layering rule: `internal/store` imports nothing from `internal/api`, `internal/engine` or `internal/jobs`, and adapters import `internal/engine` only." T019 step 7: "Do not add a store dependency to this package."
- **Impact:** An agent must either import internal/store from internal/engine/qbittorrent (forbidden by doc 03 and by the pattern T019 set) or add a callback field to client.go, which T100's Files table does not allow it to edit. The late-collision handling (pause with torrent_duplicate) additionally needs store writes the adapter cannot perform.

Remove the `sync.go` row from T100's Files table.

#### F044 · HIGH · T033 writes `tasks.requested_destination`, a column that does not exist in the DDL and that no task adds a migration for; T050/T083/T086/T109 and doc 05 rely on it too

T083 acceptance: "`requested_destination` is non-null when they differ." T017: "Do NOT add a migration".

#### F045 · HIGH · Create-time file selection for BitTorrent has no implementer: T029's Add ignores AddRequest.SelectFiles, T033 passes priorities through an undefined Extra key, and doc 03 s6.1's add-stopped -> filePrio -> start sequence appears in no task

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T033-uploads-subfolder-and-selection.md:113`, `docs/tasks/T033-uploads-subfolder-and-selection.md:114`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:149`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:197`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:104`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:105`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:761`, `docs/03-architecture.md:209`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T033 step 7: "fill `AddRequest.SelectFiles`, and pass the priority map through `AddRequest.Extra` only when the routed engine declares `per_file_priority`". doc 06: "Extra map[string]string // engine-specific escape hatch, never surfaced in the API" (no key named anywhere). T029 step 7 lists what Add sends ("`urls` ... `torrents` ... `savepath`, `category`, comma-separated `tags`, `sequentialDownload`, and `autoTMM=false`") with no SelectFiles/filePriorities handling, and "Do NOT implement `Files` or `SetFiles`; T032 owns per-file selection". doc 03 s6.1 shows "POST /api/v2/torrents/add with stopped true and paused true ... POST /api/v2/torrents/filePrio, pipe-separated ids, priority 0 for unticked files ... POST /api/v2/torrents/start". T032 only applies selection through PATCH /tasks/{id}/files. doc 05: select_files "applied to the first multi-file manifest".
- **Impact:** A user who unticks files in the add dialog (FR-007 create-time half, the M2 exit checkpoint) gets every file downloaded: the qBittorrent adapter never reads SelectFiles, the Extra key for priorities is unnamed on both sides, and no task implements the stopped/filePrio/start choreography doc 03 describes. Using `filePriorities` at add time is only possible when the file count is known (a .torrent blob), never for a magnet.
- **Suggested fix:** Add the doc 03 §6.1 choreography to T029 (or T038): when SelectFiles/priorities are present, add with stopped=true and paused=true plus stopCondition=MetadataReceived, resolve the hash, POST torrents/filePrio with pipe-separated ids, then torrents/start. Replace the unnamed Extra key with a typed `FilePriorities map[int]int` on `engine.AddRequest` in 06 §1, and update T033 step 7 and T032's out-of-scope note to point at it.

T026's Files table (reconcile.go, reconcile_test.go, aria2/client.go; 'No other file may be modified' T026:35) never touches internal/store/tasks.go, and no task in the plan declares ListByEngineRef (grep). T026:85 'Mark a task whose handle has vanished as `error` with `engine_unavailable`' but T017:103 `Transition(ctx, id, next, code, message)` takes a task_events code, not tasks.error_code, and the only …

Doc 06 §4.3 (line 541 onward) never mentions `ftp-user`/`ftp-passwd` or Extra. grep for ftp-user|ftp-passwd|Extra across docs/06 and docs/tasks finds only T020 and T033.

#### F058 · HIGH · T020 hands every task to the engine at creation and T022's resume unpauses at the engine, bypassing the T098 admission controller

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T020-create-tasks-endpoint.md:122`, `docs/tasks/T022-task-actions-and-patch.md:93`, `docs/tasks/T022-task-actions-and-patch.md:107`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:107`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:34`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:36`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:626`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:71`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T020:122 'Per accepted URI: insert the `tasks` row through `store.Create` in state `queued` … then call `Engine.Add` and store the returned handle with `SetEngineRef`.' T022:93 '| `resume` | `Engine.Resume` | `paused` → `queued`, released by T098's admission pass |' and T022:107 'Per id, call the engine, then apply the transition'. T098:107 'Release a candidate by calling `Engine.Add` when `EngineRef` is nil, or `Engine.Resume` when it is set, then transition to `downloading`'; T098's Files (T098:34-36) modify only admission.go, admission_test.go, store/tasks.go and api/tasks_actions.go — never internal/api/tasks.go. doc 05:626 'Accepted. `201`, `state: "queued"`, `error_code: "concurrency_limit"`.'
- **Impact:** After T098 exists, CreateTasks still calls Engine.Add immediately (aria2 starts the transfer at once) and `resume` still calls aria2.unpause before setting `queued`, so max_active_* is never enforced for new or resumed tasks; the admitter only ever sees tasks whose Add failed and double-resumes the rest. FR-020/FR-021/FR-123 are unmet without rework of T020/T022.
- **Suggested fix:** Fix the creation path first: either T020 inserts rows with engine_ref NULL and never calls Engine.Add (T098's Pass then owns the first Add, and internal/api/tasks.go must be added to T098's Files table), or T020 adds with StartPaused and T098 releases with Engine.Resume. Write the chosen rule into T020 step 8, and in T022's action table replace `resume | Engine.Resume | paused -> queued` with `no engine call; paused -> queued, released by T098's admission pass` so the engine is touched exactly once, by the admitter.

#### F059 · HIGH · T024 step 7 adds emission points that live in T020's handler and T017's store, neither of which is in T024's Files table

T024's Files: tasks_events.go (create), tasks_events_test.go (create), `internal/store/events.go | modify` (T024:31), server.go (modify); 'No other file may be modified' (T024:34). The insert and the Engine.Add call are in T020's CreateTasks (internal/api/tasks.go, T020:122) and store.Create (internal/store/tasks.go); T020 itself never emits an event.
- **Impact:** The agent cannot add task.created/engine.accepted/engine.unavailable without editing files outside the table (hard rule 1), so T024's AC 'contains `task.created`' is only satisfiable by a test that writes the event itself, and production never records creation or engine acceptance.

#### F061 · HIGH · T028 step 9 uses a non-existent `testcontainers.DaemonHost(ctx)` and the wrong direction: the container must reach the host via `testcontainers.HostInternal`

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:94`, `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:95`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T028:94-95 'resolving the host with `testcontainers.DaemonHost(ctx)` and rewriting the fixture URL's host to it, plus `testcontainers.WithHostPortAccess(port)`. A URL of `127.0.0.1:<port>` is the container's own loopback and the download will hang until the subtest deadline.' Verified against the testcontainers-go v0.44.0 module source: `DaemonHost` is a method `func (p *DockerProvider) DaemonHost(ctx context.Context) (string, error)` (docker.go:1607) returning the host where the daemon exposes ports to the *test process* (typically localhost); the container-side name for host ports is `HostInternal string = "host.testcontainers.internal"` (port_forwarding.go:28), enabled by `WithHostPortAccess` (options.go:126).
- **Impact:** Following the step literally does not compile (no package-level DaemonHost), and the corrected call returns 'localhost', which inside the container is exactly the loopback the step warns about — the AddURL subtest hangs until its 120 s deadline and `make test-integration` fails.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite T028 step 9: bind the `httptest` fixture to `0.0.0.0`, pass `testcontainers.WithHostPortAccess(port)` in the container request, and rewrite the fixture URL host to `testcontainers.HostInternal` (`host.testcontainers.internal`); drop the `DaemonHost` reference entirely.

#### F102 · HIGH · T115's new aria2 ENTRYPOINT breaks T028's contract-test fixture, so T115's own `make test-integration` verification cannot pass

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:56`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:76`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:84`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:129`, `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:97`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T028:97: "Have the container `Cmd` pass `--enable-rpc`, `--rpc-listen-all`, `--dir=/downloads` and `--rpc-secret` matching the `Config.Secret` the test constructs" (no environment is set). T115 entrypoint: line 56 `echo "entrypoint: ARIA2_RPC_SECRET is empty; refusing to start ..." >&2; exit 1`; line 76 `set -- su-exec "$PUID:$PGID" aria2c "$@"` then `exec "$@" --enable-rpc ... --dir=/data ... --rpc-secret="$ARIA2_RPC_SECRET" ...` (84). T115:129 verification: `... && make test-integration`, expected `TestAria2Contract/...` PASS "against a container built from this Dockerfile". T115 Out of scope: "Do NOT change the aria2 adapter or its contract test".
- **Impact:** After T115 the image's ENTRYPOINT runs `/entrypoint.sh`, which exits 1 because the fixture never sets `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET`; the contract container never listens on 6800 and the test times out. Even with the env set, the entrypoint appends `--dir=/data` and `--rpc-secret=$ARIA2_RPC_SECRET` after the fixture's flags, and aria2's last-wins option handling overrides the test's `/downloads` directory and secret. T115 cannot edit `internal/engine/aria2/contract_test.go` (not in its Files, explicitly forbidden), so the task is blocked on its own verification.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the entrypoint compose the flags first and the caller's arguments last (`exec ... aria2c --enable-rpc --rpc-listen-port=6800 --dir=/data ... "$@"`) and accept a secret supplied via `--rpc-secret` when `ARIA2_RPC_SECRET` is empty; alternatively add `internal/engine/aria2/contract_test.go` to T115's Files table with a step that sets `Env: {"ARIA2_RPC_SECRET": <Config.Secret>}` and `--dir=/data` in the fixture, and drop the 'do not change its contract test' bullet.

#### F122 · HIGH · Moving a seeding torrent's files with fsx.Move breaks the seed; the doc 03 open question is unresolved yet T076 is not marked blocked

- **Where:** `docs/03-architecture.md:537`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:77`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:11`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:1150`
- **Kind:** ambiguity · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 03: `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION: whether extracting and moving may run while a torrent is seeding, or whether seeding is suspended for their duration. The transition table assumes they are sequential.]`. T076: `Handle transitions the task to moving, calls fsx.Move, writes tasks.content_path to the new location on success and transitions back to completed.` T076 never mentions Engine.SetLocation although doc 06 declares `set_location` for qBittorrent.
- **Impact:** For a qBittorrent task that is still seeding, renaming/copying the payload out from under the daemon makes qBittorrent report `missingFiles` (-> `error`), so every torrent whose destination differs from the engine save dir ends in error. The open question blocks T076's design but T076 is `todo`, not blocked.
- **Suggested fix:** Resolve the doc 03 open question: for an engine declaring `CapSetLocation` the move job must call `Engine.SetLocation` (qBittorrent's `torrents/setLocation` moves the files itself and keeps the torrent seeding) and use `fsx.Move` only for aria2 and yt-dlp payloads; for aria2 note doc 06:643 (changeOption dir restarts the transfer) and move only after the transfer is done. Write that branch into T076's contract, steps and acceptance criteria, state which state the handler returns to when the engine still reports seeding, and delete the NEEDS CLARIFICATION line from doc 03.

#### F126 · HIGH · T085 RecheckQuota pauses only the database row; the store cannot pause the engine and the reconciler overwrites the row with `downloading` on the next poll

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T085-ownership-filtering-and-quotas.md:61`, `docs/tasks/T085-ownership-filtering-and-quotas.md:64`, `docs/tasks/T085-ownership-filtering-and-quotas.md:96`, `docs/03-architecture.md:185`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:70`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:53`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T085: `RecheckQuota ... called by the engine-progress applier the first time tasks.total_bytes changes from NULL to a value` and `it sets state 'paused' and error_code 'quota_exceeded'`; step 4: `Call RecheckQuota from the function that applies an engine TaskInfo to a row`. doc 03: `Layering rule: internal/store imports nothing from internal/api, internal/engine or internal/jobs`. T026: `Engine reports a handle dl-tool knows | Write the normalised TaskInfo back: state, byte counters, rates, ETA.` via `Transition(ctx, id, next, code, message)`.
- **Impact:** The applier is T026's Reconciler in internal/engine/reconcile.go, which is not in T085's Files table; a store-only RecheckQuota cannot call Engine.Pause, so qBittorrent keeps downloading the over-quota torrent and the next 1 s poll transitions the row back to `downloading`. FR-122's `paused` outcome is not achievable with the listed files.
- **Suggested fix:** Move the hook into the reconciler: on the NULL->value transition it calls `store.RecheckQuota`, and when that reports exceeded it calls `Engine.Pause` and only then `Transition(..., "paused", "quota_exceeded", ...)`. Add `internal/engine/reconcile.go` to T085's Files table and T026 to its `Depends on`, and add the rule that the reconciler must not overwrite a dl-tool-initiated pause with the engine's reported state (the same rule T081's schedule parking needs).

#### F127 · HIGH · T099 ENOSPC handling contradicts the engine mapping: aria2 reports code 9 as `error`, not `paused`, cannot be unpaused, and paused tasks are outside SelectQueuedCandidates; the unit test cannot fill a filesystem

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T099-disk-space-reservation.md:104`, `docs/tasks/T099-disk-space-reservation.md:106`, `docs/tasks/T099-disk-space-reservation.md:127`, `docs/tasks/T099-disk-space-reservation.md:132`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:656`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:635`, `docs/tasks/T018-aria2-status-mapping.md:107`, `docs/02-requirements.md:400`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T099 step 7: `Handle ENOSPC from a running task by transitioning it to paused with error_code disk_full`; step 8: `Resume such a task from the next admission pass once Admits is true again`. doc 06 §4.7: `| 9 "not enough disk space available" | disk_full |` and §4.6 `| error | error |`; T018: `9 -> disk_full`. FR-048: `shall pause the task with error code disk_full ... and shall resume the task once free space is above min_free_space again`. T098: `SelectQueuedCandidates returns queued tasks`. Verification: `make lint && make test PKG=./internal/...` expecting `TestENOSPCPausesAndKeepsData`.
- **Impact:** dl-tool never writes a downloading task's bytes; aria2 does, and an aria2 download that hits ENOSPC stops with status `error`, which the reconciler writes back as `error`, so the task is never `paused` and the admission pass (queued only) never revisits it. `aria2.unpause` is not valid for a stopped/errored GID, so `Engine.Resume` cannot continue the partial file; a re-add with --continue is required. Separately, a plain unit test under `make test` cannot produce ENOSPC in t.TempDir() without a privileged tmpfs mount, so the named test is not executable as specified.
- **Suggested fix:** Define the flow end to end: the reconciler maps an engine error carrying error_code `disk_full` to state `paused` + `disk_full` (and must not overwrite it), the admission candidate set is widened to include tasks paused with `disk_full`, and release re-adds through `Engine.Add` with the same dir/out so aria2 continues from its .aria2 control file rather than `Engine.Resume`. Add internal/engine/reconcile.go to T099's Files table (or hand that half to T026/T098), keep `TestAdmitsAccountsForCommittedBytes`/`IsENOSPC` as unit tests, and move the end-to-end ENOSPC assertion into make test-integration with a size-limited tmpfs.

#### F150 · HIGH · RunContract's AddURL subtest downloads an HTTP fixture URL; the qBittorrent adapter cannot accept or download a plain HTTP URL and T038 is forbidden from changing the suite

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:54`, `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:67`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:96`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:132`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:112`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:143`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T028: "// Fixture serves the bytes an AddURL subtest downloads, so no test ever reaches a third-party host." and subtest row: "`AddURL/Progress/Pause/Resume/Remove` | `Add` returns a non-empty engine-namespaced id; `Get` reaches `downloading` and `CompletedBytes` grows". T038 step 9: "Point the contract suite's download at `enginetest.Fixture`, never at a public tracker; use a locally generated single-file torrent whose web seed is that fixture URL." T038 Out of scope: "Do NOT change `enginetest.RunContract`; T028 owns the suite and its subtest list." T029 Accepts: "`magnet:` URIs, a URL whose path ends `.torrent`, and a bare 40-hex or 64-hex infohash".
- **Impact:** RunContract's only injection point is `newEngine func(t) engine.Engine`; the subtest itself calls Add with the Fixture's HTTP URL. qBittorrent's torrents/add would try to download that URL as a .torrent file and fail, so `TestQBittorrentContract/AddURL/Progress/Pause/Resume/Remove` (required PASS in T038's Verification) cannot pass without a test double that is not the adapter. T038's 'locally generated torrent with a web seed' has nowhere to be plumbed in.
- **Suggested fix:** Extend T028's contract.go so the AddURL subtest chooses its source per engine: have `Fixture` also return a generated single-file `.torrent` whose BEP 19 `url-list` is the fixture URL, and build `engine.AddRequest{Blob: torrentBytes, BlobKind: "torrent"}` when `Has(e, engine.CapBitTorrent)` and `AddRequest{URIs: []string{url}}` otherwise. Then reduce T038 step 9 to 'no change needed — T028's fixture already serves the torrent', keeping T038 out of the suite. (Note in passing: T028:42-49 lists five subtests while doc 06 §11 lists six, adding StateNormalisationCoversEveryEngineState — a separate mismatch.)

#### F161 · HIGH · aria2 GID lifetime facts make the unqualified 'vanished handle → error' rule destructive

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:72`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:153`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:554`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:497`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T026:72: "dl-tool holds a task whose handle the engine no longer knows | Transition to `error`". Doc 17:153-154: "UNVERIFIED: whether an aria2 GID survives a daemon restart through `--save-session` / `--input-file` was not confirmed". Doc 06:554: "`Remove` | `aria2.remove`, then `aria2.removeDownloadResult`". aria2 manual `--save-session`: "Save error/unfinished downloads to FILE on exit … Downloads removed using aria2.remove() and aria2.forceRemove() will not be saved. GID is also saved with gid … local metalink file: Any meaningful GID is not saved." `--max-download-result`: "The download results are completed/error/removed downloads … When queue is full … oldest download result is removed from the front" (default 1000). SessionSerializer.cc:286-288 saves FINISHED/REMOVED results only when `force-save` is true.
- **Impact:** Verified against aria2 1.37.0: (1) completed downloads are not in the session file, so after every aria2 restart every completed dl-tool task's GID is gone; (2) `removeDownloadResult` (which dl-tool itself calls) purges the GID, so the next poll sees the handle vanished; (3) after 1000 stopped results aria2 silently drops the oldest; (4) `addMetalink` downloads come back under new GIDs. Under T026's literal rule each of these marks a healthy `completed`/`removed` task as `error engine_unavailable`. The doc 17 UNVERIFIED note is answerable: GIDs survive only for unfinished/error downloads, and never for uploaded metalinks.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite T026's rule table to match 17 §1.6: skip terminal-state rows entirely, re-submit with resume semantics for `downloading`/`seeding`/`checking`, leave `queued`/`paused` alone, and reserve `engine_unavailable` for an engine that is actually unreachable. Replace doc 17's UNVERIFIED comment with the manual text above (GIDs survive only for error/unfinished downloads, never for an uploaded local metalink) and note in 06 §4.3 that a completed or removed aria2 handle is expected to disappear.

#### F162 · HIGH · Nothing starts the Reconciler: no task wires NewReconciler/Boot/Run into the process

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:46`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:61`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:82`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:140`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T026:46-47: "NewReconciler wires the registry to the task store." T026:61-63: "Run drives Boot and then a poll loop until ctx is cancelled. It is owned by the component that started it and stops with that context." A search of every task file for `NewReconciler`, `.Boot(`, `Reconciler.Run` outside T026 returns nothing; T098:82 only says "concurrently with the reconciler" and T030:64 says the ownership filter "is supplied by the Reconciler of T026" without a call site.
- **Impact:** NFR-003 (resume after restart) and the aria2 half of FR-148 are implemented as a library no `main.go`/`server.go` edit ever instantiates, so in the shipped binary tasks never reconcile and foreign transfers are never filtered. T026's own Files table contains no `cmd/dl-tool/main.go` or `internal/api/server.go` row.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `internal/api/server.go` (or `cmd/dl-tool/main.go`) to T026's Files table plus a step: construct the Reconciler in `NewServer`, call `Boot` before the listener opens and `go Run(ctx)` under the server context per doc 17 S9/S10, with an acceptance criterion that a started server reconciles once at boot.

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:554`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:107`, `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:67`, `docs/tasks/T023-remove-task-and-data.md:68`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** Doc 06:554: "`Remove` | `aria2.remove`, then `aria2.removeDownloadResult`". T019:107 repeats it. T028:67: "`Remove(id, true)` then `Get` returns `engine.ErrNotFound`". T023:68: "Stop the task at its engine | `Engine.Pause` then `Engine.Remove`". aria2 1.37.0 `removeDownload()`: for a group not found by `findGroup` (any complete/error/removed download) it throws "Active Download not found for GID#%s"; for an active group it only calls `setHaltRequested(true, USER_REQUEST)` and returns; `RemoveDownloadResultRpcMethod` throws "Could not remove download result of GID#%s" if the result is not yet in the stopped list; `pauseDownload()` throws "GID#%s cannot be paused now" for any stopped download.
- **Impact:** Removing a `completed` or `error` task (the common case for `DELETE /tasks/{id}`) fails on the first call; removing an active task and immediately calling `removeDownloadResult` fails because the halt has not been processed yet; and `Get` right after `remove` still returns the GID with `status: removed`, so T028's `Remove → Get → ErrNotFound` cannot be met. T023's `Pause` before `Remove` also errors on every stopped task.
- **Suggested fix:** Specify the adapter algorithm in 06 §4.3 and T019: read `tellStatus.status` first; if active/waiting/paused call `aria2.remove` and poll `tellStatus` (bounded, then `forceRemove`) until `status == "removed"`, then `removeDownloadResult`; if already stopped call `removeDownloadResult` only, and treat 'Active Download not found' / 'Could not remove download result' as success. Make `Pause` a no-op for a stopped download in T019 and drop the unconditional Pause from T023 step 1.

#### F165 · HIGH · Metalink downloads produce sibling/child GIDs that §8 treats as foreign and ignores

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:547`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:1068`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:101`, `docs/tasks/T026-boot-reconciliation.md:71`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:262`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** Doc 06:547: "`aria2.addMetalink([secret], metalink, options)` … Returns an **array of GIDs**; record the first and follow `followedBy`." T019:101: "returns an array of GIDs; record the first". Doc 06:1068: "a transfer is foreign when its `engine_ref` matches no `tasks` row for that engine". T026:71: "Engine reports a handle dl-tool does not know | Ignore it. It never enters `tasks`, counts toward no limit, and is never paused, relocated or deleted." aria2 manual `aria2.addMetalink`: "This method returns an array of GIDs of newly registered downloads" (one per file, siblings with no parent); `--follow-metalink` (default true): a `.metalink` URL added via `addUri` is downloaded, then "aria2 parses it as a metalink file and downloads files mentioned in it" as new GIDs listed in the parent's `followedBy`.
- **Impact:** For an uploaded Metalink with N files only GID[0] becomes `engine_ref`; the other N-1 transfers are foreign by the §8 rule, never surface, are never paused by the schedule, never rate-limited, never deleted by `delete_data`, and are lost from `task_files`. For a `.metalink`/`.meta4` URL (router row 6) the recorded GID is the tiny metadata fetch, which goes `complete` immediately, so the task shows `completed` while the real payload downloads invisibly under foreign GIDs. No task implements the "follow `followedBy`" child correlation (only T018 declares the field), and after an aria2 restart uploaded-metalink GIDs are regenerated (manual: "local metalink file: Any meaningful GID is not …
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one and write it into 06 §4.3/§8, T019, T026 and 04 §3.3: (a) one task per returned GID (split at Add), (b) a `task_engine_refs` list per task plus a reconciler rule that treats `followedBy`/`following`/`belongsTo` children of a known GID as owned, or (c) drop Metalink from v1 and delete router row 6. Also correct 06:547 — addMetalink's GIDs are siblings, not `followedBy` children.

#### F166 · HIGH · aria2 defaults --follow-torrent=true and --enable-dht=true, so an HTTP task that serves a torrent turns the aria2 sidecar into a BitTorrent client

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:484`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:452`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:148`, `docs/decisions/0005-aria2-qbittorrent-ytdlp-engines.md:39`, `docs/tasks/T019-aria2-adapter.md:124`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** ADR-0005:39: "aria2 must therefore **not** do BitTorrent." T115:148: "Do NOT give aria2 any BitTorrent role". Neither doc 06 §4.1 (lines 484-496) nor doc 10 §5.1 (lines 452-456) nor T019's `Add` options (line 124-126) sets `follow-torrent`. aria2 1.37.0 OptionHandlerFactory.cc: `PREF_FOLLOW_TORRENT … A2_V_TRUE` and `PREF_ENABLE_DHT … A2_V_TRUE`; manual: "If true or mem is specified, when a file whose suffix is .torrent or content type is application/x-bittorrent is downloaded, aria2 parses it as a torrent file and downloads files mentioned in it. … Default: true".
- **Impact:** Router row 2 only catches URLs whose path ends `.torrent`; any `https://host/dl?id=…` that returns `application/x-bittorrent` is routed to aria2 (row 4), which then auto-starts a torrent swarm with DHT and peer listening inside the sidecar, under a foreign GID dl-tool ignores. This violates ADR-0005, the FR-022/023 infohash guarantees, the exclusive-control model, and the VPN/threat posture (unexpected P2P egress from the aria2 container).
- **Suggested fix:** Add `--follow-torrent=false --follow-metalink=false-or-decided --enable-dht=false --enable-dht6=false --enable-peer-exchange=false` to the single authoritative flag list (06 §4.1) and have 10 §5.1 and T115 reference it rather than repeat it; `--bt-enable-lpd` is already false by default so it is optional. Have T019 send `"follow-torrent":"false"` per addUri as belt and braces, and add a T028 assertion that a fixture served as application/x-bittorrent is left on disk unparsed.

T088 interface: "func (r *Router) SetMediaMatcher(m MediaMatcher)"; T088 Files: "internal/engine/router.go | edit | Point row 3's `mediaMatch` hook at the cache." T090 step 9: "call `router.SetMediaMatcher(eng.Accepts)`". T020 step 7: "call `uri.Normalize`, then `engine.Route`" (no matcher named). T069 step 10: "followed by `engine.Route(n, nil)`". A grep of docs/tasks finds no `type Router` and no `NewRouter`.
- **Impact:** T088 declares a method on a type nothing in its dependency chain (T016, T087) creates, and neither T088 nor T090 lists internal/api/tasks.go or internal/rss/match.go in its Files table, so the POST /tasks handler and the RSS rule engine keep calling Route with a nil matcher. Either way, add the call sites (`internal/api/tasks.go`, `internal/rss/match.go`) to T088's or T090's Files table with a step that passes the matcher, and fix T069:156's explicit `nil`.

#### F174 · HIGH · yt-dlp SetRateLimits contract is contradictory (T090: store and return nil; T082: return ErrNotSupported) and the T028 SpeedLimitRoundTrips subtest cannot pass or be skipped for yt-dlp

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T090-ytdlp-engine-registration.md:92`, `docs/tasks/T082-live-per-task-rate-limits.md:52`, `docs/tasks/T082-live-per-task-rate-limits.md:53`, `docs/tasks/T082-live-per-task-rate-limits.md:97`, `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:70`, `docs/tasks/T028-engine-contract-test-suite.md:58`, `docs/tasks/T090-ytdlp-engine-registration.md:141`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T090: "SetRateLimits stores the download value for the next spawn. A running process is never re-limited ... The upload direction is always ignored." T082: "yt-dlp: recorded for the next spawn; a running process is never re-limited, so this returns ErrNotSupported and the caller records the value only" and acceptance "yt-dlp's `ErrNotSupported` yields `200`". T028: "`SpeedLimitRoundTrips` | `SetRateLimits(ctx, id, &1048576, nil)` succeeds and the daemon reports 1048576 for that task" with skipping keyed on "Has reports whether e declares c". T090 acceptance: "`make test-integration` runs `TestYtdlpContract` and it passes".
- **Impact:** T082 (M6) is implemented before T090 (M7) and hard-codes ErrNotSupported semantics for ytdlp, while T090 tells the adapter to return success. Whichever the adapter does, the shared suite fails: with success there is no daemon to read 1048576 back from; with ErrNotSupported the subtest fails and cannot be skipped because doc 06 defines no capability that gates SetRateLimits (doc 05's `per_task_limits` is not in the section 1 enum). T090's integration acceptance is therefore unreachable and DoD rule 6 blocks the task.
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one semantic and propagate it. Preferred: add `CapRateLimits Capability = "rate_limits"` to doc 06 section 1, declared by aria2 and qBittorrent and not by yt-dlp; make yt-dlp's `SetRateLimits` return `engine.ErrNotSupported` (T090 corrected, T082 unchanged, T090's Capabilities acceptance updated); add `CapRateLimits -> SetRateLimits` to T028 step 6's table so `SpeedLimitRoundTrips` skips and `UnsupportedCapabilityReturnsErrNotSupported` covers it. If instead yt-dlp returns nil and stores, correct T082 and make T028's subtest read the value back through a capability-gated path. Record the decision in doc 06 section 10.1.


### M2 — BitTorrent via qBittorrent

#### F022 · HIGH · User URIs handed to aria2/yt-dlp/qBittorrent bypass the SSRF guard via DNS rebinding and redirects; NFR-017 Verify claims coverage T122 cannot provide

- **Where:** `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:111`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:149`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:153`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:64`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:72`, `docs/tasks/T122-apply-ssrf-guard-to-user-uris.md:57`, `docs/tasks/T122-apply-ssrf-guard-to-user-uris.md:110`, `docs/tasks/T029-qbittorrent-session-and-add.md:149`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** doc 12 §2.2: "A resolve-then-hand-the-hostname-to-the-client design is a DNS-rebinding TOCTOU bypass". doc 12 rule 2: "Every outbound fetch — task URIs, `.torrent` fetches, ... — uses this one client." doc 12 rule 6: "a `302 → http://169.254.169.254/` must never pass." T122: "PreflightURI validates a user-submitted URI before any engine is asked to fetch it, because the engines dial for themselves and the dialer guard of ssrf.go never sees their connections." T122 step 3: "otherwise `r.LookupNetIP(ctx, "ip", host)`". T029 step 7: "Implement `Add` as `POST torrents/add` ... `urls` for" (qBittorrent fetches the .torrent URL itself). doc 02 NFR-017 Verify: "a public host that redirects to `127.0.0.1` are all blocked ... T122 asserts the same for a URI submitted to `POST /tasks`."
- **Impact:** For every task URI the actual fetch is done by aria2 (http/ftp/sftp), yt-dlp (media) or qBittorrent (`.torrent` URLs via `urls=`), each with its own resolver and redirect following. PreflightURI is exactly the resolve-then-hand-off pattern doc 12 marks IMPORTANT as a bypass: a rebinding DNS name (public answer for the preflight, 169.254.169.254 / RFC1918 at connect time) or a plain `302 → http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` on a public host defeats it and lands the metadata/LAN response as a file in /data. NFR-017's Verify line claims T122 asserts redirect blocking for POST /tasks, which T122's test list does not (and cannot) include, so the requirement is marked implemented while the …
- **Suggested fix:** Do not adopt fix (a) as written: pointing qBittorrent's proxy setting at a dl-tool CONNECT proxy also routes tracker and peer traffic through it and breaks BitTorrent. Instead: (1) in T029/doc 06 section 5.3, fetch `.torrent` URLs inside dl-tool with the T123 guarded client and send the bytes as a `torrents` file part rather than `urls=` (that lane is free, the multipart part already exists); (2) add an engine-egress row to the doc 12 section 1 boundary table and a paragraph to section 2.3 recording the residual risk for aria2/yt-dlp fetches (attacker-controlled redirect or short-TTL rebind lands a LAN/metadata response in /data), pointing at the `vpn` profile and a compose-level deny of …

#### F120 · HIGH · Multi-torrent file selection: doc 09 §5 pages a selection per torrent, but POST /tasks applies `select_files` only to the first multi-file manifest

T033: "FileSelectionRequest ... is applied to the first multi-file manifest of the submission." T049 `onCreate: (draft, selection: {index, selected, priority}[])` — a single flat selection.
- **Impact:** Selections made on pages 2..n are silently discarded by the server; T049's single-array `onCreate` cannot even carry them. The M2/M3 promise that a per-torrent selection step works is false for any batch of more than one torrent.

FR-090 as experienced through the UI (T118) is broken: a new limit takes effect only after a restart or the next cell change.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a step to T092 (or a small follow-up task) so that `PatchSettings`, after `PutSettings` commits any of `download_rate_limit`, `upload_rate_limit`, `alt_download_rate_limit`, `alt_upload_rate_limit` or `schedule_enabled`, calls `Governor.LoadAndApply`/`ApplyMode`; add T079 to T092's `Depends on`, add the governor handle to the handler construction (server.go is already in T092's Files; main.go must be added or the governor passed through the existing handler struct), and name that caller in T079's contract.

#### F125 · HIGH · Admission ignores the schedule: tasks added during a No Download cell are released by T098 because ApplyMode is a one-shot no-op while the mode is unchanged

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T081-schedule-evaluation-and-alternative-speed.md:100`, `docs/tasks/T081-schedule-evaluation-and-alternative-speed.md:99`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:107`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:1148`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T081 step 4: `make ApplyMode a no-op when the mode is unchanged, so the minute tick is idempotent.` T081 step 5: `Implement ModeNoDownload as a pause of every task in downloading, checking or queued that dl-tool started`. T098 step 5: `Release a candidate by calling Engine.Add when EngineRef is nil, or Engine.Resume when it is set, then transition to downloading`. doc 06: `Every task dl-tool started is paused.`
- **Impact:** T098's Pass never consults Governor.Mode(), and T081 only parks the tasks that exist at the moment the cell flips. Any task created (POST /tasks, RSS grab, watch folder) while the cell is 0 sits in `queued`, is released by the next admission tick and downloads through the No Download window — FR-093 fails for exactly the common case.
- **Suggested fix:** Gate admission on the schedule: pass the governor into the Admitter (or a `mode func() Mode` accessor) and make `Pass` release nothing while `schedule_enabled` is true and `Mode() == ModeNoDownload`, parking any queued candidate through `ScheduleParked` instead. Alternatively make `EvaluateSchedule` re-park newly queued tasks on every minute tick while the mode is ModeNoDownload rather than only on a change. State the chosen rule in T081's steps and add an acceptance criterion: a task created inside a `0` cell stays paused/parked.
