#### F149 · HIGH · T038 fallback adds the magnet with stopped=true and stopCondition=MetadataReceived — a stopped torrent never fetches metadata, so the fallback always times out

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:46`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:82`, `docs/tasks/T038-magnet-metadata-and-contract-call-site.md:102`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** "Fallback, used when either endpoint answers 404 or 405: add the magnet with stopped=true, paused=true\n// and stopCondition=MetadataReceived, poll torrents/files until it answers, then remove the handle"; step 4: "Implement the fallback exactly as described: add with `stopped=true`, `paused=true` and `stopCondition=MetadataReceived`, poll `torrents/files` every 500 ms".
- **Impact:** In qBittorrent a torrent added stopped performs no DHT/tracker/peer activity; the metadata-received alert never fires, so `stopCondition=MetadataReceived` (which stops the torrent *after* metadata arrives) is meaningless when combined with stopped=true (the GUI disables the stop-condition control when 'Start torrent' is unchecked). On any daemon where fetchMetadata is unavailable, InspectMagnet always returns ErrMetadataTimeout and the inspect flow degrades to metadata_pending for every magnet. `torrents/files` also returns an empty 200 list (not an error) before metadata, so 'until it answers' is not a usable condition.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite T038:46-47 and step 4: the fallback adds the magnet started (`stopped=false`, `paused=false`) with `stopCondition=MetadataReceived`, polls `torrents/info?hashes=<hash>` every 500 ms for `has_metadata: true` (a live 5.2.3 field per evidence.md) rather than treating an empty `torrents/files` 200 as 'not answered yet', then reads `torrents/files`, then deletes the handle with `deleteFiles=true` on every exit path. Keep the deadline and `ErrMetadataTimeout` as specified.

#### F168 · HIGH · Argv never enables node with --js-runtimes; yt-dlp enables only deno by default, so the probed DLTOOL_JS_RUNTIME_PATH is never used

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:947`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:1035`, `docs/tasks/T087-ytdlp-subprocess-runner.md:113`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:81`, `docs/tasks/T113-ytdlp-pin-and-capability-probe.md:55`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06 section 7.1 argv: "--no-colors", "--newline", "--no-playlist", "--paths", "--output", "--download-archive", "--progress-template", "--print-to-file", "--no-simulate", uri — no runtime flag. doc 06 line 1035: "The runtime image ships **`nodejs`**, not deno". doc 11 line 81: DLTOOL_JS_RUNTIME_PATH is "JavaScript runtime required by `yt-dlp-ejs`" and is read by runner.go only for the `--version` probe (T113 step 5).
- **Impact:** Since the 2025.09 releases yt-dlp's `--js-runtimes RUNTIME[:PATH]` option enables runtimes, and only deno is enabled by default; node, bun and quickjs must be opted in explicitly. The plan installs node, probes node, but never tells yt-dlp to use node, so on the pinned build yt-dlp searches PATH for deno, finds nothing, warns that no supported JavaScript runtime is available, and YouTube n/sig challenges fail — exactly the failure the whole js_runtime_missing machinery exists to prevent, and it happens with the probe reporting MediaEnabled == true.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `--js-runtimes node:<DLTOOL_JS_RUNTIME_PATH>` to the 06 §7.1 argv and to T087's Argv (no `--no-js-runtimes` is needed since deno is absent from the image; the option parses cleanly on 2026.08.19). Have T113 paste `yt-dlp --help | grep -A8 -- --js-runtimes` under Evidence, and make the boot probe assert the enabled runtime rather than only the binary's `--version`.

#### F169 · HIGH · yt-dlp extractor patterns are Python re dialect ((?x), lookarounds, conditionals); Go regexp rejects them, so the 'drop on compile failure' rule silently drops YouTube

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T088-ytdlp-extractor-cache.md:100`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:960`, `docs/tasks/T088-ytdlp-extractor-cache.md:106`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T088 step 4: "Compile each pattern with `regexp.Compile`, cap the input at 4096 bytes per pattern, and drop — with one `warn` log line naming the extractor — any pattern that fails to compile. One bad pattern never fails the load." doc 06 section 7.2: "make `Accepts(uri)` a regexp match against that cache".
- **Impact:** Even if the enumeration flag existed (V7), yt-dlp _VALID_URL strings use verbose mode `(?x)` with embedded comments, negative lookahead `(?!...)`, lookbehind and conditional groups `(?(1)...)` (YoutubeIE does), none of which RE2/Go regexp supports. Those extractors would compile-fail and be dropped per step 4, so youtube.com URLs would route to aria2 and download an HTML page, while the load reports success. T088's acceptance criteria and T090's registration would all pass.
- **Suggested fix:** Do not feed yt-dlp `_VALID_URL` strings to Go `regexp`. Combine with V7 (no enumeration flag exists): build a host/path allow-list asset at image build time from `gen_extractor_classes()` and match on host, or answer `Accepts` with an offline `yt-dlp --simulate --use-extractors default,-generic` probe (evidence.md: 1.1 s, no network on rejection). Delete the compiled-regexp cache from doc 06 §7.2 and T088, and if any regexp survives, make a pattern that fails to compile a loud, counted failure rather than a warn-and-drop.

#### F171 · HIGH · AddRequest.Filename and Rename are handed to yt-dlp verbatim as the -o output template: absolute or ../ values escape --paths and the user jail, and % sequences expand

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:99`, `docs/tasks/T087-ytdlp-subprocess-runner.md:115`, `docs/tasks/T090-ytdlp-engine-registration.md:89`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:214`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:199`
- **Kind:** security · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06 AddRequest: "Filename string // rename / output-template override". T087 Argv: "--output <req.Filename or DefaultOutputTemplate>". T090: "Rename is supported: it rewrites the -o template for the next spawn." doc 12 section 3.1 "Where the names come from" lists Content-Disposition, .torrent names, BEP 47 and API destination — not the yt-dlp output template.
- **Impact:** yt-dlp joins --paths home with the -o template via os.path.join, so an absolute template ignores --paths entirely and `..` segments walk out of SaveDir; the template language also expands %(field)s and a stray % makes yt-dlp abort with an invalid-template error. A non-admin user who can PATCH a task name (or submit filename) can therefore write into another user's jail or anywhere /data is writable, bypassing the fsx validation that only SaveDir received. The `--print-to-file FILE` argument is likewise an output template, so a destination folder containing % breaks the info document path.
- **Suggested fix:** In T087's Argv, pass Filename through 12 §3.2 `sanitiseSegment`, reject any path separator, escape `%` as `%%`, and append `.%(ext)s` so the template is one segment relative to `--paths`; apply the same to T090's Rename and percent-escape `infoJSONPath`. Add `/etc/x`, `../x` and `%(id)s` cases to T087's runner_test.go and add the yt-dlp output template as a row in 12 §3.1.

#### F172 · HIGH · .dl-tool-info.json is one file per SaveDir and --print-to-file appends, so concurrent tasks collide and a Pause/Resume respawn leaves a multi-line file ParseInfoDocument cannot decode

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:973`, `docs/tasks/T087-ytdlp-subprocess-runner.md:55`, `docs/tasks/T089-ytdlp-progress-and-exit-codes.md:82`, `docs/tasks/T090-ytdlp-engine-registration.md:124`, `docs/tasks/T090-ytdlp-engine-registration.md:126`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06: "the final info document written to `<task-dir>/.dl-tool-info.json` via `--print-to-file`". T087: "const InfoJSONName = \".dl-tool-info.json\"" and Argv "--print-to-file %()j <infoJSONPath>". T089: "ParseInfoDocument decodes <SaveDir>/.dl-tool-info.json." T090 step 6: "`Resume` as a fresh `Spawn` with the same `AddRequest`". T090 step 7: "delete the recorded output file only when `deleteData` is true."
- **Impact:** yt-dlp's --print-to-file appends a line per invocation ("Append given template to the file"). Two media tasks sharing the default destination write the same file and read each other's name, ContentPath and size; a Pause then Resume of one task appends a second JSON line, so json.Unmarshal of the whole file fails and Files/Name/ContentPath are lost after every resume. Remove never deletes the info file, the per-task archive file or .part fragments, so /data accumulates dotfiles and /config/archives grows without bound.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the info document per task and outside the user's destination — `<DLTOOL_CONFIG_DIR>/ytdlp/<ulid>.json` — set that path in `Runner.Spawn` (`Proc.InfoPath`), truncate it before each spawn (or decode with `json.Decoder` and keep the last object), and update T089:82's stated path. Add a step to T090's `Remove` that deletes the info file and the archive file unconditionally and `.part`/fragment files when `deleteData` is true.

#### F173 · HIGH · Progress status 'finished' is mapped to completed and progress.filename to ContentPath, but both are per-format-file values: completion fires before merge/post-processing and names the temporary .fNNN file

- **Where:** `docs/06-download-engines.md:988`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:989`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:999`, `docs/tasks/T089-ytdlp-progress-and-exit-codes.md:132`, `docs/tasks/T089-ytdlp-progress-and-exit-codes.md:127`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:952`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 06 field table: "`downloading` → `downloading`; `finished` → `completed`" and "`filename` | The final filename (**always present**) | `ContentPath`". T089 step 8: "emitting ... `engine.EventCompleted` for `finished`". doc 06 line 952 argv: "--print-to-file", "%()j", infoJSONPath (no WHEN prefix); T089 step 6: "set `ContentPath` from `Filename`".
- **Impact:** yt-dlp's download progress hook runs once per downloaded file: for a bestvideo+bestaudio selection it reports finished twice (video, then audio) with filename set to `<title>.f137.mp4` / `.f140.m4a`, and the merged output only exists after the FFmpegMerger post-processor runs; for a playlist it fires per entry. T089 therefore marks the task completed while audio is still downloading and records a temp file as ContentPath that ffmpeg later deletes, which breaks the M6 post-processing move/extract and delete-data flows. The `--print-to-file` without a WHEN prefix fires at the `video` stage (before download), so `filename` there is the prepared name, not the post-processed `filepath`.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat `finished` as a progress event only and derive completion from `ClassifyExit(0)` after `Wait`; change the argv to `--print-to-file after_move:%()j <path>` and read `filepath` (falling back to `filename`) for ContentPath; only fall back to progress.filename when no info document exists. Update the 06 §7.3 status/filename rows and T089 steps 6 and 8 together, and add a two-format fixture to parse_test.go asserting no completion on the first `finished`.

#### F175 · HIGH · With the JS runtime missing, T113 makes Accepts return false so media URLs route to aria2 and download an HTML page — a silent failure that contradicts doc 06 section 7.6, and the task_events row of step 9 is unreachable

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T113-ytdlp-pin-and-capability-probe.md:112`, `docs/tasks/T113-ytdlp-pin-and-capability-probe.md:114`, `docs/tasks/T113-ytdlp-pin-and-capability-probe.md:128`, `docs/tasks/T113-ytdlp-pin-and-capability-probe.md:73`, `docs/06-download-engines.md:1046`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T113 step 8: "When `MediaEnabled` is false, make `Accepts` return false so the router falls through to aria2"; acceptance: "With the media lane disabled, a media URL routes to aria2 rather than being rejected." T113 step 9: "Emit one `task_events` row with code `js_runtime_missing` ... the first time a media submission is refused for that reason". doc 06 section 7.6: "A missing JS runtime never fails a download silently and never crashes the process".
- **Impact:** Routing youtube.com/watch?v=... to aria2 produces a completed task whose content is the watch page's HTML — the definition of failing silently — and because the router never calls ytdlp.Add, the refusal path that writes the js_runtime_missing task_events row and the ErrUnavailable-with-warning contract on Health/Add never execute. The error_code js_runtime_missing that doc 04 reserves for exactly this case is never set on any task.
- **Suggested fix:** Split the two failure modes in T113: `Accepts` returns false only when the yt-dlp binary itself is absent (Health `ErrUnavailable`); with the binary present but no JS runtime, keep `Accepts` true and have `Add` fail so the API records the task in state `error` with `error_code`/task event `js_runtime_missing`. Additionally raise the `js_runtime_missing` event once at boot as doc 06 §7.6 and doc 11 §283 specify, rather than only on first refusal, and rewrite T113 step 8 and acceptance bullet 5 to match.


### M3 — web UI

#### F018 · CRITICAL · SSE liveness rule contradicts the server's push cadence, and heartbeat comments are invisible to EventSource

- **Where:** `docs/09-web-ui-spec.md:855`, `docs/tasks/T051-event-stream-and-reconnect.md:82`, `docs/tasks/T051-event-stream-and-reconnect.md:96`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:653`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:690`, `docs/09-web-ui-spec.md:862`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 09:855: "On the first missed heartbeat, more than twice the 1 s tick, the connection dot turns amber and the grid dims to 70 % opacity." T051:82: "| no message for more than 2 s | `connection = 'offline'`, dot amber, grid dimmed to 70 %. |" T051:96: "Track liveness from the payloads and the heartbeat comment: more than 2 s without either means offline." versus doc 05:653: "The server pushes at most once per second, and only when something changed." and doc 05:690: "Emit a comment line every 15 seconds while idle (`send.Comment("hb")` → the wire bytes `: hb`)". doc 09:862 (rule 6): "While disconnected, mutating controls are disabled with the tooltip `Reconnecting…`".
- **Impact:** Implemented literally, a quiet server (nothing changed for 2 s) makes the client declare itself offline, dim the grid, show the banner and disable every mutating button — the app is unusable at idle. Independently, the browser EventSource API never surfaces comment lines, so 'track liveness from the heartbeat comment' cannot be implemented in the SPA at all; only a real `sync` event (or an `event: hb` with data) is observable.
- **Suggested fix:** Make doc 05 §6.1 the single home for the cadence and specify a client-observable keepalive: emit a named `event: hb` (empty data) every second while idle in addition to `: hb` for proxies, and keep T051's >2 s liveness rule; otherwise raise the client threshold above the 15 s comment interval and delete the comment-based liveness sentence. Update doc 09 §10.8 rule 1 and T051 lines 82 and 96 to whichever cadence is chosen (and align T051's `POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 2000` with doc 09:858's 3 s poll while there).

(doc 09's change log says those open questions were dropped and "removed the `localStorage` fallback for the grid layout"). T107's Files table lists only `internal/api/prefs.go`, `internal/api/watchfolders.go`, `internal/api/prefs_test.go`, `internal/store/settings.go`, `internal/api/server.go` — no `web/` file. `useUiPrefs.ts` is edited only by T045, T048, T049, T053, T064 (none in M6). T064 (M4): "both controls read and write only through the `useUiPrefs` store", acceptance "Reloading the …
- **Impact:** In M4 the only persistence `useUiPrefs` has is localStorage (T045), which T064 forbids, and `GET /prefs` does not exist until T107 in M6, so T064's acceptance criterion and its Playwright reload case cannot pass as written. Beyond T064, FR-144's client half (doc 09 §3.3 server-side prefs, grid layout shared across browsers) is never built by any task: T053 attributes it to T107, which is server-only.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `web/src/store/useUiPrefs.ts` to T107's Files table (or create a small client task after T107) that hydrates from `GET /prefs` on boot, writes `PUT /prefs` on the existing 500 ms debounce and keeps `localStorage` as the first-paint mirror; delete T045's stale reference to doc 09's open questions; reword T064's acceptance criterion and out-of-scope bullet to say persistence goes through the `useUiPrefs` store (localStorage in M4, `/prefs` from M6).

#### F068 · HIGH · T039's verification grep for external origins fails on React's own production bundle

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T039-ui-stack-and-design-tokens.md:137`, `docs/tasks/T039-ui-stack-and-design-tokens.md:131`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T039:137 `cd web && npm ci && npm run build && ! grep -rEq "https?://[a-z]" dist/index.html dist/assets && ... && echo UI_STACK_OK`; T039:131 `npm run build emits an index.html and bundles referencing no http:// or https:// origin.`
- **Impact:** react-dom's production build embeds `https://react.dev/errors/` (the minified-error decoder URL) and `http://www.w3.org/2000/svg` (the SVG namespace used by createElementNS); lucide-react adds the same xmlns string once any icon is imported. The grep therefore always matches, `!` inverts it to non-zero, the `&&` chain stops and `UI_STACK_OK` can never be printed, so the task's Verification block cannot pass as written and the acceptance box cannot be ticked.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the bundle-wide scan with a load-oriented check, e.g. `! grep -rEq '(src|href)="https?://|url\(https?://|@import[^;]*https?://' dist/index.html dist/assets/*.css`, and keep an assertion that every `<script>`/`<link>` in dist/index.html is relative. Reword T039:131 to 'loads no script, stylesheet, font or icon from an external origin' and align doc 02 NFR-022's Verify line with it.

#### F069 · HIGH · T039 mandates `shadcn add drawer` and `sonner` while forbidding the packages those copy-ins install (vaul, next-themes)

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T039-ui-stack-and-design-tokens.md:114`, `docs/tasks/T039-ui-stack-and-design-tokens.md:152`, `docs/tasks/T039-ui-stack-and-design-tokens.md:128`, `docs/09-web-ui-spec.md:68`, `docs/09-web-ui-spec.md:73`

- **Evidence:** T039:114 `Run npx shadcn@4.19.1 add button checkbox dialog drawer input label select popover context-menu tabs tooltip sonner`; T039:152 `Do NOT add next-themes, react-dropzone, react-hotkeys-hook, parse-torrent, zod, cmdk or vaul`; T039:128 `web/package.json carries every version string from doc 09 §1 unchanged and no forbidden package.`; doc 09:73 `cmdk and vaul are out of v1 scope; mobile sheets use shadcn/ui's drawer.`; doc 09:68 `Deliberately **not** dependencies ... next-themes → shadcn/ui's Vite dark-mode provider`
- **Impact:** shadcn/ui's `drawer` component is a wrapper around the `vaul` package and the CLI installs `vaul` as a dependency; the `sonner` copy-in installs `sonner` and, in the registry template, imports `useTheme` from `next-themes` and installs it. Every other listed primitive installs `radix-ui`/`@radix-ui/react-*`, none of which doc 09 §1 lists. Running step 5 as written adds packages the same task and doc 09 §1 forbid, so acceptance line 128 cannot be satisfied and hard rule 3 (no dependency without an ADR/pin) is broken. The agent must either skip the copy-ins (breaking later tasks that use drawer/sonner) or violate the prohibition.
- **Suggested fix:** Correct doc 09 §1 (lines 68-74) and T039: `vaul` and `sonner` are transitive copy-in dependencies of the drawer and toast primitives, not optional — either allow them explicitly (pinned at implementation time, recorded in the §1 table) and require the copied `sonner.tsx` to read the theme from `web/src/lib/theme.ts` instead of `next-themes`, or drop `drawer` and `sonner` from step 5 and name their replacements (`dialog`/sheet + a hand-written toast). Amend T039:128/152 to match, and list `radix-ui` (doc 09:41 already assumes 'Radix default') in the §1 table.

The 'four' count is also wrong (five members). Adjacent to the known finding that T049 needs `rememberLastDestination` before T053 defines it.

#### F076 · HIGH · Playwright spec ordering/parallelism makes T104's `make e2e` unrunnable: login specs need an admin that only setup.spec.ts may create

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T043-playwright-harness-and-grid-performance.md:69`, `docs/tasks/T043-playwright-harness-and-grid-performance.md:106`, `docs/tasks/T043-playwright-harness-and-grid-performance.md:123`, `docs/tasks/T104-accessibility-harness.md:99`, `docs/tasks/T103-progressive-web-app.md:41`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T043:69 `fullyParallel: false,` (no `workers`, no project `dependencies`, no globalSetup); T043:106 `first run creates the admin navigates to /, expects the redirect to /setup, fills the token from readSetupToken() plus ADMIN, submits, and expects the app shell`; T104:99 `For each entry of SCREENS, sign in where required with loginAsAdmin`; T104 adds `web/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` and `web/e2e/keyboard.spec.ts`; T103 adds `web/e2e/pwa.spec.ts`; T043:123 `Re-running make e2e twice in a row passes both times.`
- **Impact:** `fullyParallel: false` only serialises tests inside one file; separate files still run in parallel workers, and with one worker they run alphabetically (a11y, keyboard, pwa, search, setup). `loginAsAdmin` in a11y/keyboard needs an admin, which does not exist until setup.spec.ts runs; if those specs call `ensureAdmin` first, setup.spec.ts's `first run creates the admin` then finds setup already complete (409) and `readSetupToken()` times out because the token file was deleted on success. Either way the suite is non-deterministic or red once T104 lands, so the M3 exit 'Playwright E2E green' is unreachable.
- **Suggested fix:** In T043's config add `workers: 1` and split the projects: a `setup` project with `testMatch: /setup\.spec\.ts/` that the `chromium` project names in `dependencies`, so the wizard spec always runs first on the freshly wiped state directory; make the remaining specs call `ensureAdmin` before `loginAsAdmin`. State in T103 and T104 that their specs run in the dependent project, and — since doc 13:255 requires axe on the setup wizard while `/setup` redirects away once an admin exists — have T104 analyse the wizard inside the `setup` project (or against its own fresh state directory) rather than from `SCREENS`.

T072: "the item actions are **Download selected**, **Mark read**, **Mark all read**" and step 8 "Treat read state as server-derived: render `read` as it arrives and re-fetch after `Mark read`; do not keep a local read set." T065: "Read state has no column in doc 04 §3.5, so it is derived, not stored: an item is **unread** when `first_seen_at >= feeds.last_success_at`" and "Do NOT add a `read` column, a `read_items` table". doc 05 §2 lists only `GET·POST·PATCH·DELETE /feeds[/{id}]`, `POST /feeds/{id}/refresh`, `GET /feeds/{id}/items` — no item mutation.

#### F145 · HIGH · T046's `SanitiseSegment(s string) string` cannot satisfy the doc 12 §3.4 table it must implement verbatim; row 28 also contradicts §3.3 rule 2

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T046-filesystem-roots-and-browse.md:51`, `docs/tasks/T046-filesystem-roots-and-browse.md:122`, `docs/tasks/T046-filesystem-roots-and-browse.md:138`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:214`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:219`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:271`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:282`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:243`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T046: `func SanitiseSegment(s string) string` and acceptance "`TestSanitiseSegmentTable` runs all thirty rows of doc 12 §3.4 and every one passes." doc 12 §3.2 step 2: "Percent-decode only when the source was `filename*`." Row 6: "`..%2F..%2Fetc%2Fshadow` from `filename*` | decodes to `../../etc/shadow` → **reject the download**". Row 17: "`badname`, and **reject** when it came from a user-typed path". Row 29: "BEP 47 symlink entry pointing at `/etc` | **reject; never create a symlink**". Row 30: "`Movie.mkv`, `movie (2).mkv`" (collision map of §3.3 rule 7 — not in any T046 function). Row 28: "torrent `name = "/"` | `_` | absolute name" versus §3.3 rule 2: "**reject the whole download** if it is `".."` or absolute".
- **Impact:** The function has neither a source parameter (needed by steps 2 and 5 and rows 6/17) nor an error return (rows 6, 17, 27, 29 expect rejection), the collision suffixing of row 30 and the BEP 47 handling of row 29 belong to a torrent-metainfo consumer that T046 does not touch, and row 28 expects `_` where rule 2 demands rejection. A literal implementation cannot make all thirty rows pass, so the acceptance criterion is unsatisfiable and the agent will either block or weaken the table.
- **Suggested fix:** In T046 change the contract to `SanitiseSegment(s string, src Source) (string, error)` with `Source` in {ContentDisposition, ContentDispositionExt, TorrentPath, UserTyped} (or split into Sanitise + Validate), and rewrite acceptance to 'every row of section 3.4 marked SanitiseSegment passes'. In doc 12 section 3.4 add an 'Owner' column: rows 3, 27 -> SafeJoin (T046); rows 29, 30 -> the metainfo consumer (T031, whose step at T031:136 already runs segments through sanitiseSegment) and the torrent writer that owns the collision map; all others -> SanitiseSegment. Resolve row 28 to 'reject the whole torrent' so it matches section 3.3 rule 2 and section 3.1, or amend rule 2 to exempt `info.name`.

#### F158 · HIGH · T063 uses the TanStack Query v4 `refetchInterval(data)` signature while v5.102.8 is pinned

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T063-search-screen.md:125`, `docs/tasks/T039-ui-stack-and-design-tokens.md:53`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T063 step 1: "`refetchInterval: (data) => (data?.finished ? false : 1000)`"; T039: ""@tanstack/react-query":"5.102.8"". In v5 the callback receives the `Query` object (`(query) => ...`), not the data.
- **Impact:** `data?.finished` reads `query.finished` (undefined) so the interval is always 1000 ms and polling never stops; `TestPollStopsWhenFinished` (acceptance line 150) fails as written.
- **Suggested fix:** Write `refetchInterval: (query) => (query.state.data?.finished ? false : 1000)` in T063 step 1, and check the same file for any other v4-era callback shapes (e.g. `onSuccess`/`onError` on useQuery, removed in v5).


### M4 — search

#### F025 · CRITICAL · Every provider URL shape in doc 07 §2.6 uses a port the mandated SSRF client rejects (80/443 only)

- **Where:** `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:246`, `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:249`, `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:250`, `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:397`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:152`, `docs/tasks/T123-ssrf-guarded-http-client.md:85`, `docs/tasks/T055-indexer-store-and-provider-wizard.md:198`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 07 §2.6: "| Jackett, single indexer | `http://127.0.0.1:9117/api/v2.0/indexers/<indexer-id>/results/torznab/api` | default port 9117 |", "| Prowlarr | `http://prowlarr:9696/<indexerId>/api` |", "| bitmagnet | `http://bitmagnet:3333/torznab/api` | no API key |"; doc 07 §3.5: "| Ports | 80 and 443 only by default | |"; doc 12 §2.2 rule 5: "Allow ports 80 and 443 only; a URL naming any other port is rejected before the dial."; T123: "Ports 80 and 443 are the only ones permitted."; T055 step 8 only sets "`allow_private_network: true`, because a provider on the Compose network is private." — nothing lifts the port rule, and doc 11 defines no port setting.
- **Impact:** The primary tier (ADR-0008: Prowlarr/Jackett/bitmagnet 'for free') is unreachable through the only permitted http.Client: the provider wizard, `t=caps` on create (T055 step 6 returns 403 ssrf-blocked), every search and every test against the documented URLs fail. Doc 07's 'by default' implies an override that no document defines (rule 4 of AGENTS.md: every port is a setting in doc 11).
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one mechanism and write it in all three places: make the per-indexer private-network flag (see F156) also lift the 80/443 restriction for that indexer's fetches — i.e. `NewGuard(log, allowPrivate)` gains an allowed-port set that is {80,443} by default and unrestricted (or a configured list) when allow-private is on — or add `DLTOOL_SSRF_ALLOW_PORTS` to doc 11 §3. Thread it through T123 `Check`/`NewGuard` and its tests, into the client used by T054/T055/T058/T061, and restate doc 12 §2.2 rule 5, doc 12 §2.3 and doc 07 §3.5 to say exactly the same thing; doc 07 §2.6 keeps its URLs.

#### F085 · CRITICAL · Feed body cap and total timeout contradict: doc 08/T066 say 16 MiB and 60 s with a raw LimitReader, doc 12 §2.4/T123 say 8 MiB via secure.ReadCapped and a 120 s client

- **Where:** `docs/08-rss-automation.md:66`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:65`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:105`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:107`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:120`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:185`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:183`, `docs/tasks/T123-ssrf-guarded-http-client.md:54`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** doc 08:66 "| Max response size | 16 MiB |"; doc 08:65 "| Timeout | connect 10 s, total 60 s |"; T066:105 `maxBody = 16 << 20 // 16 MiB`; T066:120 "read the body through `io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody+1)`"; doc 12:185 "| Body, metadata fetch (`.torrent`, feed, indexer page) | 8 MiB, enforced while streaming |"; doc 12:183 "| Connect timeout · total request timeout | 10 s · 120 s |"; T123:54 "// MetadataFetchCap is the body cap for a .torrent, feed or indexer fetch: 8 MiB."; doc 08:522 feed 8 "9.2 MB — the 16 MiB cap and streaming-parser test."
- **Impact:** Two authoritative documents give different caps for the same fetch. T066 re-implements the cap with a bare LimitReader instead of T123's ReadCapped (which also rejects a lying Content-Length up front, doc 12 rule 7), and the 9.2 MB fixture feed the plan uses as its cap test passes under one document and is rejected under the other. The 60 s total timeout cannot be applied to the shared client (Timeout 120 s) without a per-request context deadline, which no step mentions.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the cap one home: keep doc 12 §2.4 authoritative and have doc 08 §2.1 link to it, then set both to the same value (8 MiB from `secure.MetadataFetchCap`, or raise `MetadataFetchCap` to 16 MiB if the 9.2 MB fixture must pass with headroom). Change T066 step 2 to `secure.ReadCapped(resp, secure.MetadataFetchCap)` so the Content-Length pre-check applies, adjust the oversize test and the error text, and state that the 60 s total is a `context.WithTimeout` around each poll.

#### F156 · HIGH · Per-indexer allow_private_network is stored but never plumbed into a guard/client; every M4 call site receives one global client

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T055-indexer-store-and-provider-wizard.md:198`, `docs/tasks/T061-async-search-jobs.md:143`, `docs/tasks/T058-dlsearch-runner-and-probe.md:84`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:172`, `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:274`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T055: "The settings document also carries `allow_private_network: true`, because a provider on the Compose network is private."; T061: "idx *store.IndexerStore, hc *http.Client) Handler" (one client); T058: "func NewRunner(hc *http.Client, log *slog.Logger, userAgent string) *Runner"; doc 12: "| Per-indexer `allow_private` flag on an `indexers` row | Lifts the same set for that indexer's fetches only"; doc 07: "a Prowlarr or Jackett engine record requires `allow_private_network: true`. This is the only routine reason to set that flag."
- **Impact:** No task reads `settings_json.allow_private_network` to build a `secure.NewGuard(log, true)` client for that indexer, so a Prowlarr/Jackett on the RFC 1918 compose network is blocked unless `DLTOOL_SSRF_ALLOW_PRIVATE=true` globally — which doc 11 tells the user to leave false. The flag is also named `allow_private` in doc 12 and `allow_private_network` in doc 07/T055.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a `ClientFor(row store.Indexer) *http.Client` helper (one cached guarded client per allow-private value) to T055 and use it in T055 step 6/8, T058 `Probe`/`Search` and T061's handler instead of a single `hc`; correspondingly amend T122:169 and T123:168 (which currently forbid the per-indexer flag) and relax T122's acceptance line 145 to allow the helper's construction site. Unify the name on `allow_private_network` in doc 12 §2.3 and state once, in doc 07 §3.1, whether the flag lives on the indexers row's settings_json, on the definition, or both.

#### F157 · HIGH · `response.fields` is decoded into a Go map, but templates depend on declaration order

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T056-dlsearch-definition-loader.md:102`, `docs/tasks/T058-dlsearch-runner-and-probe.md:151`, `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:361`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T056: "Fields map[string]Field `yaml:"fields"`"; T058 step 8: "Resolve fields in declaration order so `{{ .Result.<field> }}` can see earlier ones"; doc 07 §3.3: "`{{ .Result.<field> }}` | a field already resolved for the current row; fields resolve in declaration order". The bundled internet-archive and academic-torrents definitions rely on this (`_id` before `title`/`details`/`download`, `_hash` before `download`).
- **Impact:** Go map iteration order is randomised; `{{ .Result._id }}` in `download` will randomly be evaluated before `_id` is resolved, so two of the four bundled engines produce empty download URLs nondeterministically and `TestJSONRowExtraction` flakes.
- **Suggested fix:** In T056 change `Response.Fields` to an order-preserving type — e.g. `Fields []NamedField` with a custom `UnmarshalYAML` that walks the `yaml.Node` mapping content pairs (and rejects a duplicate key) — keep a `map[string]Field` index for lookup if needed, and have T058 step 8 iterate the slice. Add a T056 test asserting the decoded order of internet-archive's nine fields matches the file.

#### F159 · HIGH · Imported definitions (.dlm conversions, uploaded .dlsearch.yaml) have no runtime home: Registry loads only embedded + /config/engines, and T059 forbids writing there

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T059-dlm-static-import.md:127`, `docs/tasks/T059-dlm-static-import.md:165`, `docs/tasks/T057-bundled-engine-definitions.md:80`, `docs/tasks/T061-async-search-jobs.md:143`, `docs/07-search-and-indexers.md:852`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T059 step 8: "store the inert source and `Origin` in `settings_json`" (the converted `Definition` is not persisted); T059: "Do NOT write the uploaded archive or any member to `/config` or `/data`."; T057: "SeedIndexers creates one indexers row per bundled definition" and `NewRegistry(log, userDir)` loads only `definitions.FS` plus `userDir`; T061: "func NewSearchHandler(db *sqlx.DB, log *slog.Logger, reg *search.Registry, run *search.Runner, ..." resolves dlsearch rows through `reg`.
- **Impact:** After a user enables an imported engine, the search handler and `POST /indexers/{id}/test` cannot obtain its `*Definition` (not in the Registry), so FR-053's converted engines can never run. Likewise a hand-written `/config/engines/*.dlsearch.yaml` gets a Registry entry but no task creates its `indexers` row (`definition_source='user'` is never written), so it cannot be enabled either.
- **Suggested fix:** Decide one home for an imported definition and document it in doc 04 §3.4 and doc 07 §7: either a `definition_yaml` member of `settings_json` or a new nullable column, with `definition_id` set to the imported engine's id. Add a step to T059 that writes it, and a `Registry` seam (e.g. `Registry.Overlay(id string, yaml []byte)` or resolution through the IndexerStore) that T058's Probe and T061's handler consult before falling back to the bundled/user set. Separately, extend `SeedIndexers` (or add a step in T061) to create `definition_source='user'` rows for definitions found in the user directory.


### M5 — RSS

#### F132 · CRITICAL · Global poll interval default and minimum contradict across doc 08, doc 11, T065, T066, T092 and T117

- **Where:** `docs/08-rss-automation.md:60`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:61`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:143`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:90`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:91`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:121`, `docs/tasks/T117-rss-settings-section.md:91`, `docs/tasks/T092-settings-and-system-info.md:129`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** doc 08:60 "| Global poll interval | **30 min** |"; :61 "| Minimum allowed interval | **5 min** |" (and "Reproduce these values exactly"). doc 11:143 "| `rss_interval_s` | integer seconds, minimum `60` | `900` | `PATCH /settings` |". T066:90-91 "EffectiveInterval is max(configured, ttlMinutes*60, impliedIntervalS, 300) seconds, where configured is feeds.refresh_interval_s or, when that is 0, the rss_interval_s setting." T065:121 "`refresh_interval_s` below `300` and not `0`" → 422. T117:91 "Edited in minutes, sent in seconds, minimum 1 minute." T092:129 "`rss_interval_s` at least `60`".
- **Impact:** Two authoritative documents disagree on the default (1800 s vs 900 s) and the floor (300 s vs 60 s). PATCH /settings accepts 60–299 s, the settings screen lets an admin type 1–4 minutes, and the poller silently clamps to 300 s, so the saved value is not what runs; per-feed overrides are rejected below 300 while the global is accepted at 60.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the numbers only in doc 11 §5 and have doc 08 §2.1 link to it. Choose one pair (e.g. default 1800 s, minimum 300 s) and align doc 11:143, doc 08:60-61, T065:121, T092:129 and T117:91-102 (`min={5}` minutes) to it, so the floor in `EffectiveInterval` equals the documented minimum instead of hiding a clamp.

#### F081 · HIGH · T066 sets Accept-Encoding by hand, which turns off Go's transparent gzip decoding; nothing decompresses the body before ParseFeed

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:104`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:120`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:124`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:72`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:102`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T066:104 `acceptEnc = "gzip, deflate"`; T066:120 "read the body through `io.LimitReader(resp.Body, maxBody+1)`"; T066:124 "On `200`: call `p.parser.ParseFeed`"; doc 08:72 "| `Accept-Encoding` | `gzip, deflate` |". net/http Transport documentation: "If the Transport requests gzip on its own and gets a gzipped response, it's transparently decoded in the Response.Body. However, if the user explicitly requested gzip it is not automatically uncompressed."
- **Impact:** Every feed served with Content-Encoding gzip or deflate (most CDN-hosted feeds) reaches gofeed as compressed bytes, ParseFeed fails, the feed climbs the backoff ladder and the M5 checkpoint feed never yields items. Deflate is never handled by net/http at all. The 16 MiB cap is also applied to compressed bytes, not the decoded document.
- **Suggested fix:** Either delete `acceptEnc` from T066's constants and the `Accept-Encoding` row from doc 08 §2.1/§2.3 so the Transport negotiates and decodes gzip itself, or add a T066 step that switches on `resp.Header.Get("Content-Encoding")` and wraps the body in `gzip.NewReader`/`flate.NewReader` before the cap, with the cap applied to the decoded stream, plus a `poll_test.go` case serving a gzip-encoded fixture.

#### F082 · HIGH · T065 counts new rows from RowsAffected of an INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE, which SQLite reports as 1 for updated rows too, so the idempotency test and items_added cannot be right

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:104`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:129`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:150`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:124`, `docs/tasks/T071-commit-grabs-and-run-rule.md:138`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T065:104 "INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (feed_id, identity) DO UPDATE SET title=..., updated_at=... and returns how many rows were new"; T065:129 "counting new rows from the `RowsAffected` of inserts that did not conflict"; T065:150 "`TestUpsertFeedItemsIsIdempotent` asserts a second upsert of the same batch adds `0` rows"; T071:138 "a `200` that added items calls `RunAllRules`".
- **Impact:** In SQLite the DO UPDATE branch counts as a changed row (sqlite3_changes() = 1), so RowsAffected is 1 for every item whether inserted or updated. Followed literally, the second upsert reports len(items), TestUpsertFeedItemsIsIdempotent fails, POST /feeds/{id}/refresh reports items_added = total items on every poll, and T071's post-poll rule pass runs on every 200 even when nothing is new.
- **Suggested fix:** Name the counting statement explicitly in T065's interface contract: either `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (feed_id, identity) DO NOTHING` (RowsAffected 0/1, summed) followed by a separate UPDATE of `title`/`updated_at` for the conflicting identities, or keep the upsert and count with `RETURNING (created_at = updated_at)`; state which one in the `UpsertFeedItems` comment and in step 3.

#### F083 · HIGH · T067 tells the agent to read the unprefixed <infohash>/<size> elements through gofeed's extensions map, but gofeed never puts unprefixed elements there (they land in Item.Custom)

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T067-item-parser-and-uri-extraction.md:101`, `docs/tasks/T067-item-parser-and-uri-extraction.md:130`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:143`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T067:101 "Read `<infohash>` and `<size>` by **local name** through gofeed's extensions map: Academic Torrents declares `xmlns:academictorrents` yet emits both elements unprefixed."; T067:130 "`TestSynthesiseMagnetFromInfohash` asserts the exact magnet string above." gofeed v1.3.0 internal/shared/extparser.go: `func IsExtension(p) bool { ... return !(prefix == "" || prefix == "rss" || prefix == "rdf" || prefix == "content") }`; rss/parser.go item loop: `if shared.IsExtension(p) { ... item.Extensions = ext } else if name == "title" ... else { ... item.Custom[p.Name] = result }`.
- **Impact:** An agent that searches it.Extensions for "infohash"/"size" finds nothing for the Academic Torrents fixture: tier C source 3 never fires, TestSynthesiseMagnetFromInfohash fails, size_bytes stays NULL, and the doc 08 §9 golden item cannot be reproduced. (Verified against the gofeed v1.3.0 source downloaded from proxy.golang.org; unprefixed unknown elements are stored in `Item.Custom` keyed by the raw element name.)
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite T067 step 2 to read `it.Custom["infohash"]` and `it.Custom["size"]`, noting that the key is the element's original-case name and that Custom keeps only the last occurrence of a repeated element; keep `it.Extensions` for prefixed `torznab:`/`media:` sources only. Mention the same split in doc 08 §3.1's note about unprefixed elements.

T070 `DryRunItem`: `MatchedBy map[string]string` — no offset member.

Otherwise drop requirement 3 from doc 08 §8 and step 4 from T073.

#### F135 · HIGH · gofeed's universal Feed/Item cannot supply `guid/@isPermaLink`, `<ttl>`/`<skipHours>`/`<skipDays>`, or element order, which T067's contract requires

- **Where:** `docs/08-rss-automation.md:140`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:154`, `docs/tasks/T067-item-parser-and-uri-extraction.md:69`, `docs/tasks/T067-item-parser-and-uri-extraction.md:111`, `docs/tasks/T066-feed-poller-and-backoff.md:58`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** doc 08:140 "Library: `github.com/mmcdole/gofeed`"; doc 08:154 tier D "then `<guid>` when `isPermaLink != \"false\"`"; T067:69 "func ExtractDownloadURI(it *gofeed.Item, baseURL string) (uriStr, infoHash string, tier Tier, ok bool)"; T067:111 "Fill `FeedMeta` from `<title>`, `<ttl>` clamped to `[5,1440]` **minutes**, `sy:updatePeriod` and `sy:updateFrequency`, `<skipHours>` and `<skipDays>`." Checked on pkg.go.dev (2026-09-02): `gofeed.Feed` has no `TTL`, `SkipHours` or `SkipDays` field and `gofeed.Item.GUID` is a plain `string`; only `gofeed/rss.Feed` has `TTL string, SkipHours []string, SkipDays []string` and `gofeed/rss.Item.GUID` is `*GUID{Value, IsPermalink}`. Neither model records document order between `<enclosure>` and `<link>` (`Link`/`Links []string` vs `Enclosures []*Enclosure`).
- **Impact:** With the signature as written, tier D's `isPermaLink` test and step 7's `<ttl>`/`<skipHours>`/`<skipDays>` cannot be implemented from `*gofeed.Item`/`*gofeed.Feed`; tier A's "document order, last wins" between an `x-bittorrent` enclosure and a `magnet:` `<link>` (`TestTierALastWins`) cannot be derived from either gofeed model at all.
- **Suggested fix:** In T067, parse with `gofeed.Parser{KeepOriginalFeed: true}` and fill `FeedMeta` and tier D's `isPermaLink` from `feed.OriginalFeed().(*rss.Feed)` (items stay in the same order), and change `ExtractDownloadURI` to take the format-specific item plus the item's raw XML so tier A's document-order last-wins can be decoded with `encoding/xml`. Pin `github.com/mmcdole/gofeed v1.4.2` in T004 since `KeepOriginalFeed` does not exist in v1.3.0, and record the approach in doc 08 §3.

#### F136 · HIGH · Web-page-only items: doc 08 tier D keeps any `<link>` (and §9 expects Linuxtracker items stored), while FR-072 and T067 require such an item to be discarded

- **Where:** `docs/08-rss-automation.md:154`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:177`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:547`, `docs/02-requirements.md:525`, `docs/02-requirements.md:527`, `docs/tasks/T067-item-parser-and-uri-extraction.md:122`, `docs/tasks/T067-item-parser-and-uri-extraction.md:131`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 08:154 "| **D** | `<link>` (RSS) / `link[@rel=\"alternate\"]/@href` or the first `<link>` (Atom) ... | Last resort. |"; doc 08:177 "An item that yields no URI at all is discarded"; doc 08:547 (feed 4, whose `<link>` is a details page) "identity falls to the download URI, and the 40-hex `id` query parameter is recognised as an info-hash by `(?i)\\b[0-9a-f]{40}\\b`". doc 02:527 "T067 parses fixtures for all three shapes plus a web-page-only item and asserts three extractions and one discard." T067:122 "an item with only a details page and no hash is discarded"; T067:131 "`TestItemWithoutURIIsDiscarded` asserts the item is absent from the returned slice."
- **Impact:** Under tier D every item with a `<link>` yields a URI, so Linuxtracker/Gutenberg/LibriVox items are stored with an HTML page as `download_url` (and later routed to aria2 as an HTTP download), while FR-072's verify line and T067 require exactly that item to be discarded — both cannot hold. The `(?i)\b[0-9a-f]{40}\b` URL scan §9 expects is in neither the §3.1 ladder nor the §3.2 identity chain, so no step produces it.
- **Suggested fix:** Decide in doc 08 §3.1 whether tier D accepts any `<link>` or only a download-looking one (`.torrent`, `magnet:`, or a URL carrying a 40/64-hex hash); adopt the latter, add the hash-in-URL scan as an explicit tier C source with the `(?i)\b[0-9a-f]{40}\b` pattern, and then align FR-072's verify line, §9's expectations for feeds 4/5/6 and T067 steps 3, 5 and 9 with the choice.

#### F137 · HIGH · T065's `Feed` struct hides seven members (`json:"-"`) that doc 05 §10.1 returns and T072 consumes, and has no `unread_count`

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:59`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:60`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:61`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:62`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:63`, `docs/tasks/T065-feed-store-and-crud.md:64`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:956`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:957`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T065:59-64 `LastFetchAt ... json:"-"`, `LastSuccessAt ... json:"-"`, `NextFetchAt ... json:"-"`, `EscalationLevel ... json:"-"`, `DisabledTill ... json:"-"`, `LastError ... json:"-"`. doc 05:956-958 "\"enabled\":true,\"refresh_interval_s\":0,\"item_cap\":50,\"unread_count\":3, \"last_fetch_at\":\"2026-09-01T09:30:00Z\",\"last_success_at\":...,\"next_fetch_at\":...,\"escalation_level\":0,\"disabled_till\":null,\"last_error\":null". T072:54-55 "unread_count: number; last_error: string | null;".
- **Impact:** Serving `store.Feed` as the wire shape (which its `json` tags invite) omits seven members doc 05 promises and T072 needs (the `⚠` state and tooltip from `last_error`, the unread pill from `unread_count`); it would also emit unix-millisecond integers where doc 05 §1.6 requires RFC 3339 strings.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a `FeedResponse` DTO to T065's interface contract in `internal/api/feeds.go` carrying exactly doc 05 §10.1's member set with RFC 3339 timestamps, plus a store function (or an extra `ListFeedsWithCounts`) that computes `unread_count` from the `first_seen_at >= last_success_at` predicate; drop the `json` tags from `store.Feed` so it can never be served directly.

#### F139 · HIGH · Unique partial index on `rule_matches.info_hash` makes T071's fallback/failed rows and its own duplicate-info-hash test impossible

- **Where:** `docs/04-data-model.md:436`, `docs/tasks/T071-commit-grabs-and-run-rule.md:131`, `docs/tasks/T071-commit-grabs-and-run-rule.md:144`, `docs/tasks/T069-rule-matching-algorithm.md:80`, `docs/08-rss-automation.md:450`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 04:436 "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_rule_matches_hash ON rule_matches(info_hash) WHERE info_hash IS NOT NULL;". T071:131 "Insert losers with `status = 'fallback'` and the same `content_key`, so a failed hand-off can be retried with the runner-up."; T071:144 "a duplicate info hash across two feeds creates one task". T069:80 "ContentKey ... else the info hash". doc 08:450 "`rule_matches.info_hash` (unique partial index) for the grab".
- **Impact:** Two feeds announcing one release share the info hash; with T069's content key they form one group, the winner row is inserted, and the `fallback` row with the same `info_hash` violates the unique index so `Commit` errors — the exact scenario T071 step 9 tests. The index also blocks any retry of a `failed` row (no second `queued` row for that hash) and a `rejected` row coexisting with a `sent` one.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the index conditional on live rows — `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON rule_matches(info_hash) WHERE info_hash IS NOT NULL AND status IN ('queued','sent')` — or drop uniqueness to a plain index and enforce "one row in (queued,sent) per info_hash" inside `Commit`; state in T071 that `fallback` and `failed` rows keep their `info_hash`, and update T071's context note (line 27) accordingly.

Neither T106 nor T077 lists a migration file in its Files table, and doc 14 §8.3 requires a migration plus a doc 04 DDL update in the same commit, so the implementing agent is blocked by hard rule 1.
- **Suggested fix:** Add 'last_send_at INTEGER' and 'last_error TEXT' to the notification_channels DDL in docs/04-data-model.md §3.2 (all tasks are still 'todo' and T006 transcribes §3 into 00001_init.sql, so no second migration is needed), or delete both members from doc 05 §14's example, T106's ChannelView and T120's ChannelRow and drop the last_error write from T077 step 4. Record the choice in doc 04 §3.2, which owns the DDL.

#### F088 · HIGH · doc 10 §3.5 (T076's cited context) prescribes a different move helper than T076's contract: umask-masked modes vs hard-coded 0644/0755, dst.part+fsync vs staging-dir+size-verify, and `error` vs `paused` on failure

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:276`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:281`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:286`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:287`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:23`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:53`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:54`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:83`
- **Kind:** contradiction · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 10 §3.5 'EXDEV-aware move helper (mandatory)': '4. Preserve mode and mtime; never hardcode 0644/0755 — open with 0666/0777 and let UMASK mask it.' and '2. On EXDEV ... stream src → dst.part, fsync the file, close, rename(dst.part, dst) within the destination directory, fsync the destination directory, then unlink src.' and '5. ... on failure leave the source intact and mark the task error.' T076 (which lists doc 10 as context item 2): 'files at 0644 and directories at 0755', 'Do NOT copy or preserve ownership, setuid bits, extended attributes or symlinks; files land at 0644 and directories at 0755', '3. verify: every destination file exists with the same size as its source', 'Do NOT hash file contents to verify; size equality per file is the documented check', and 'A refusal pauses the task with error_code disk_full'.
- **Impact:** The task file and the document it tells the agent to read disagree on three implementable facts (file modes, the copy/durability mechanism, the failure state). Whichever the agent follows, the other authoritative text is violated; doc 10's fsync-before-unlink durability rule is dropped entirely by T076's size-only verification.
- **Suggested fix:** Reconcile in one place: make doc 10 section 3.5 a short paragraph linking to T076's contract as the owner. Pick umask-masked 0666/0777 (consistent with ADR-0011's UMASK=002 contract) over the hard-coded 0644/0755, require fsync of each copied file and of the destination directory before the staging rename and before unlinking src, and state the failure states once: paused + disk_full for space exhaustion (FR-045), error for everything else.

#### F090 · HIGH · The mandatory startup mount/hardlink self-check (doc 10 §3.4, doc 17 §1.4) has no owning task, and its `hardlinks_available` result has no home in the `/system/info` schema

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:263`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:269`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:274`, `docs/17-operations-and-runbook.md:106`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:1268`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:1277`, `docs/tasks/T076-exdev-aware-move.md:11`

Record `hardlinks_available` per root so the move helper can pick its strategy". Doc 17 §1.4 repeats the S7 stage. A grep of every docs/tasks/T*.md for `hardlinks_available`, `linkprobe`, `st_dev` or `self-check` returns nothing; T076 (the move helper) does not mention it; the `GET /system/info` example in doc 05 §13 has no `hardlinks_available`/roots member.
- **Impact:** A stage the plan labels mandatory in two documents is never built: no boot probe, no banner, no `hardlinks_available` field for the move helper or the diagnostics bundle (doc 17 §7 `boot-checks.json`). The doc 17 §8 rows 'A completed task sits in moving...' and 'Hardlinks unavailable although...' point operators at a banner that does not exist.
- **Suggested fix:** Give S7 an owner: add `internal/fsx/selfcheck.go` + `selfcheck_test.go` to T076's Files table (or a new M6 task) implementing doc 10 §3.4 - per-root `st_dev` compare, real link probe, never fail the boot - with a test that fakes a failing `link()` on a same-`st_dev` pair; add `"roots":[{"path":"/data","hardlinks_available":true}]` to the `GET /system/info` example in docs/05-api-contract.md §13 and to T092's Files/Steps and acceptance criteria; add the persistent banner to the status-bar task (T044) and list `boot-checks.json` content as fed by that result in doc 17 §7.

#### F124 · HIGH · Notification fan-out only runs for the post-processing chain's terminal event; `["*"]`, `task.error` and `disk space low` are never delivered

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T077-notification-delivery.md:119`, `docs/04-data-model.md:637`, `docs/05-api-contract.md:1323`, `docs/tasks/T120-users-auth-and-notifications-settings.md:128`, `docs/02-requirements.md:373`
- **Kind:** missing · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T077 step 8: `Edit internal/jobs/postprocess.go to call Fanout with the chain's terminal event.` (the only production caller of Fanout in any task). doc 04 §4.8: `event_mask is a JSON array of task_events.code values; ["*"] selects every code.` doc 05 §14 example: `"event_mask":["task.completed","task.error"]`. T120 matrix lists `'task.error'`. FR-045 requires `emit a disk space low notification event`.
- **Impact:** Engine errors, quota pauses, disk_full, schedule pauses and every other task_events code are written to task_events but never handed to Notifier.Fanout, so a channel masked `["*"]` or `["task.error"]` receives only chain-terminal events. The UI matrix (T120) offers codes that can never fire.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the task_events insert the single fan-out point: give the store a completion callback (the layering rule at 03-architecture.md:185 forbids internal/store importing internal/jobs, so it must be a func field or channel set at wiring time), have T077 register the notifier on it and list the store file and the wiring file in T077's Files table, and keep the chain's terminal event as one ordinary event insert rather than a separate path. Add an acceptance criterion that a `task.error` written by `Transition` reaches a stub channel masked `["*"]`.

#### F128 · HIGH · `.tgz`/`.gz` tarballs cannot be extracted in one 7zz pass under the no-recursion rule

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T074-auto-extract-archives.md:127`, `docs/tasks/T074-auto-extract-archives.md:132`, `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:348`, `docs/02-requirements.md:667`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T074 step 6: `Never recurse into a nested archive.` step 9: `build one fixture of each of the six formats ... assert each extracts`. doc 12: `Never recurse — extract one level and leave nested archives alone.` FR-100 lists `.zip, .tar, .gz, .tgz, .rar and .7z`.
- **Impact:** 7-Zip models gzip as a single-member container: `7zz x file.tgz` yields `file.tar`, not the files; unpacking the tar is a second, nested extraction (7-Zip's own idiom is `7zz x -so a.tgz | 7zz x -si -ttar`). With recursion forbidden, `.tgz` and `.tar.gz` payloads are 'extracted' to a bare .tar and the acceptance criterion `One archive of each of ... .tgz ... extracts successfully` cannot be met without violating the recursion rule.
- **Suggested fix:** State in doc 12 section 4.1 and T074 that gzip/bzip2/xz-over-tar is ONE logical archive: run `7zz x -so <archive> | 7zz x -si -ttar -o<tmp>` (or use archive/tar + compress/gzip for the tar family and keep 7zz for zip/rar/7z), with pass-1 listing done as `7zz l -slt -ttar -si` on the decompressed stream or the caps enforced purely by bytes written; add that `.gz` of a non-tar single file is one member and stays a single pass; keep 'never recurse' for genuinely nested archives (a .zip inside a .zip).

#### F144 · HIGH · T075 says a wrong password is distinguished from a corrupt archive by the 7zz exit status; 7-Zip returns exit code 2 for both

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T075-extraction-password-list.md:78`, `docs/tasks/T074-auto-extract-archives.md:93`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T075: "A candidate that fails is distinguished from a corrupt archive by the `7zz` exit status: a wrong password is retried with the next candidate, an invalid archive aborts the whole job at once with `ErrInvalidArchive`."
- **Impact:** 7-Zip's exit codes are 0 OK, 1 warning, 2 fatal error, 7 command-line error, 8 out of memory, 255 user break; 'Wrong password' and 'Data Error' / 'Can not open the file as archive' all end in 2 (fatal). An implementer following T075 cannot make `TestExhaustedListSetsWrongPassword` and `TestTruncatedArchiveIsInvalid` both pass; whichever mapping they choose, one class of failure gets the wrong error_code and the candidate loop either aborts early or wastes every candidate on a corrupt file.
- **Suggested fix:** Specify stderr-based discrimination in T075: capture the child's stderr and classify exit 2 as ErrWrongPassword when it contains `Wrong password` (7-Zip prints `ERROR: Wrong password : <name>`, or for formats without a password check `Data Error in encrypted file. Wrong password?`), and ErrInvalidArchive otherwise (`Can not open the file as archive`, `Unexpected end of archive`, `Data Error`). Optionally add the cheaper pre-check: pass 1 already runs `7zz l -slt`, so read `Encrypted = +` and run `7zz t -p<candidate> -y <archive>` per candidate before pass 2. Keep the rule that no candidate ever reaches a log line.

#### F625 · HIGH · T086's orderCandidates operates on []store.Task, but T098's admitter works on []engine.Candidate, which carries no added_at; and nothing plumbs process_order into the Admitter

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T086-users-and-default-destinations.md:97`, `docs/tasks/T086-users-and-default-destinations.md:105`, `docs/tasks/T086-users-and-default-destinations.md:124`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:66`, `docs/tasks/T098-concurrency-limiter.md:74`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed (severity or scope adjusted)
- **Evidence:** T086:105 "func orderCandidates(in []store.Task, order ProcessOrder) []store.Task" with T086:97-103 "OrderByDateCreated → added_at ascending / OrderByUserRoundRobin → one task per owner in added_at order ... ties broken by added_at". T098:66-67 "// SelectQueuedCandidates returns queued tasks in process_order, oldest added_at first.\n\tSelectQueuedCandidates(ctx context.Context, limit int) ([]Candidate, error)" and T098:74 "type Candidate struct {\n\tID string\n\tOwnerID string\n\tEngine string\n\tEngineRef *string\n}". T098 step 2: "Add `SelectQueuedCandidates` ordered by `added_at ASC, id ASC`". T086 step 7: "Edit `internal/engine/admission.go` to read `process_order` and apply `orderCandidates` before the limit checks".
- **Impact:** The type the admitter actually holds is engine.Candidate, not store.Task, so orderCandidates as specified does not compile against T098's code; engine.Candidate has no added_at field, so neither documented ordering rule is computable; and Admitter.Pass(ctx, Limits) receives only the three max_active_* values, so admission.go has no way to read the process_order setting (Limits has no member for it and package engine has no settings access). T098 also already claims the ordering lives in the store's SQL, so two tasks own the same fact.
- **Suggested fix:** In T086 restate the helper as 'func orderCandidates(in []Candidate, order ProcessOrder) []Candidate' operating on the already added_at-ordered slice, add a 'ProcessOrder ProcessOrder' field to engine.Limits (declared in admission.go, which T086 may modify) and say explicitly that the load func passed to Admitter.Run reads the process_order setting — naming the task that owns that wiring, since no task currently calls NewAdmitter/Run. Alternatively move the ordering into SelectQueuedCandidates, delete orderCandidates and add internal/store/tasks.go to T086's Files table; either way fix T098's AdmissionStore comment so only one document owns the ordering.


### M7 — media downloads, packaging, release

#### F100 · HIGH · `vpn` profile cannot start: gluetun and profile-less qbittorrent both publish 6881, and the override's `ports: []` does not clear qbittorrent's ports (Compose needs `!reset`)

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:68`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:130`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:177`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:627`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:633`, `docs/tasks/T094-harden-compose-and-release-verification.md:60`, `docs/tasks/T094-harden-compose-and-release-verification.md:163`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** doc 10:68 header comment: `COMPOSE_PROFILES=aria2,vpn,proxy docker compose up -d`; qbittorrent ports (130-131): `- "6881:6881"` / `- "6881:6881/udp"`; gluetun ports (177-178): the same two lines (T094:60-61 copies them). doc 10 §8.1:627: "an override file the operator saves next to `compose.yaml` and applies with `docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.vpn.yaml --profile vpn up -d`"; line 633: `ports: [] # a container in another netns cannot publish ports`. T094 verification (163) only runs `make compose-check` (`docker compose config -q`).
- **Impact:** (1) With the `vpn` profile active and no override, both qbittorrent and gluetun bind host 6881/tcp+udp, so the header-comment command fails with "port is already allocated"; `docker compose config` does not detect host-port collisions, so T094's verification passes while `up` fails. (2) Compose merges sequence attributes across files; an empty `ports: []` in the override leaves qbittorrent's two entries in place. The spec's own example for removing them is `ports: !reset []`. As written, qbittorrent keeps `ports` plus `network_mode: service:gluetun`, and the Engine rejects it with "conflicting options: port publishing and the container type network mode" — the very error the doc quotes. (3) …
- **Suggested fix:** In doc 10 §8.1 write `ports: !reset []` (and note the Compose >= 2.24 requirement); state that the `vpn` profile is only valid together with the override file and amend the doc 10:68 header comment to `docker compose -f compose.yaml -f deploy/compose.vpn.yaml --profile vpn up -d` (or drop `vpn` from that example); add `deploy/compose.vpn.yaml` to T094's Files table and extend T094's verification with `docker compose -f compose.yaml -f deploy/compose.vpn.yaml --profile vpn config | yq '.services.qbittorrent.ports'` asserting it is empty.

#### F142 · HIGH · Host allowlist has no configuration variable; the shipped Caddy/Traefik profiles use dl.example.com and would receive 421 on every request

- **Where:** `docs/12-security-and-threat-model.md:516`, `docs/tasks/T095-proxy-hardening-and-headers.md:60`, `docs/tasks/T095-proxy-hardening-and-headers.md:61`, `docs/02-requirements.md:1071`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:523`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:524`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:551`, `docs/11-config-reference.md:72`

NFR-013: "a `Host` header outside the configured allowlist". doc 11 §2 defines no allowed-hosts variable (grep for ALLOWED_HOSTS/TRUSTED_HOSTS/host allowlist across docs/ returns nothing). doc 10 §7.1: "dl.example.com {\n\treverse_proxy dl-tool:8080 {" (Caddy passes the original Host by default); §7.2 Traefik rule `Host(`dl.example.com`)`.
- **Impact:** The implementer of T095 has no way to populate `extra` (hard rule 4: every setting must be defined in doc 11), so the always-on allowlist admits only localhost and literal IPs. Every proxied deployment by DNS name — the exact topology doc 10 ships and T095 step 10 tests behind Caddy — answers 421 Misdirected Request for all traffic.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `DLTOOL_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (`,`-separated hostnames, default empty, `infrastructure`, owner `internal/api/security.go`) to docs/11-config-reference.md §2, and note in §8 that an unparseable value is `config_malformed`; parse it into `cfg.AllowedHosts []string` in T005 (add the field and a test); add doc 11 §2 to T095's context list and pass `cfg.AllowedHosts` into `HostAllowlist` in T095 step 7, with a test case asserting a configured name passes and an unconfigured one is 421; mention the variable beside the Caddy and Traefik snippets in doc 10 §7.1/§7.2 and add it to `.env.example` (doc 10 §6 and T125). If a database-backed setting is preferred instead, it must be added to doc 11 §5 and …

#### F163 · HIGH · Compose healthcheck for aria2 always fails: an unauthenticated aria2.getVersion returns HTTP 400 and curl -f treats that as failure

- **Where:** `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:154`, `docs/10-deployment-and-compose.md:156`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:99`, `docs/tasks/T115-aria2-image-build-and-publish.md:114`
- **Kind:** technical-inaccuracy · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** Doc 10:154-156: "# Liveness only: proves the JSON-RPC listener answers HTTP. It carries no token on purpose … test: [\"CMD-SHELL\", \"curl -fsS -X POST -d '{…\\\"method\\\":\\\"aria2.getVersion\\\",\\\"params\\\":[]}' http://127.0.0.1:6800/jsonrpc >/dev/null\"]". T115:99-100: "`curl` is present only so the compose healthcheck can post `aria2.getVersion`". aria2 1.37.0 `HttpServerBodyCommand::sendJsonRpcResponse`: "case 1: // error caught while executing RpcMethod\n httpCode = 400;" — the `Unauthorized` failure is a code-1 error, and `aria2.getVersion` requires the token (only `system.listMethods`/`system.listNotifications` are exempt, doc 06:523).
- **Impact:** With `-f`, curl exits 22 on the 400, so the `aria2` service is reported `unhealthy` forever on every deployment, and T115 step 9's expectation that an unauthenticated call is "answered at the transport level" is misleading. Any later `depends_on: condition: service_healthy` (e.g. the VPN override pattern in §8.1) would never start.
- **Suggested fix:** Change the doc 10 §3 healthcheck body to a method that is exempt from the token - `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":"hc","method":"system.listMethods"}` returns HTTP 200 unauthenticated - keeping `curl -fsS`; alternatively keep `getVersion` and pass `"params":["token:$ARIA2_RPC_SECRET"]` (CMD-SHELL already allows the interpolation). Reword T115 step 9 and its acceptance criterion to assert HTTP 200 from the chosen probe and a `{"code":1,"message":"Unauthorized"}` body for a token-requiring method, and add a `docker inspect --format '{{.State.Health.Status}}' aria2` check to T115's verification.

#### F603 · HIGH · T087's Proc exposes neither the stdout reader nor the stderr buffer, so T090 cannot read progress or classify the exit

- **Where:** `docs/tasks/T087-ytdlp-subprocess-runner.md:70`, `docs/tasks/T087-ytdlp-subprocess-runner.md:91`, `docs/tasks/T087-ytdlp-subprocess-runner.md:136`, `docs/tasks/T090-ytdlp-engine-registration.md:118`, `docs/tasks/T089-ytdlp-progress-and-exit-codes.md:104`
- **Kind:** executability · **Status:** confirmed
- **Evidence:** T087: `// Spawn starts one process … Stdout is returned as a line reader for T089; stderr is captured into a bounded buffer.` — but the returned struct is `type Proc struct { ID string; Cmd *exec.Cmd; SaveDir string; InfoPath string; StartedAt time.Time; cancel context.CancelFunc }` and step 5 says `attach a StdoutPipe and a bounded stderr buffer of 64 KiB`, storing neither. T090 step 3: `start one goroutine that runs ScanProgress over stdout into e.events and calls ClassifyExit when the process exits`, where `ScanProgress(taskID string, r io.Reader)` and `ClassifyExit(exitCode int, stderrTail string)` both need those handles.
- **Impact:** The pipe was already consumed inside Spawn (a second `Cmd.StdoutPipe()` returns an error), and the stderr buffer is unreachable, so T090 cannot implement its central event loop. T090's Files table does not include `runner.go`, so it may not add the fields; hard rule 1 forces it to stop.
- **Suggested fix:** In T087's interface contract add `Stdout io.ReadCloser` and either `Stderr *bytes.Buffer` or a `func (p *Proc) StderrTail() string` accessor to `Proc`, and amend step 5 to say Spawn records both on the returned `Proc` (the 64 KiB bound kept on the stderr writer). No change is needed in T090 once the handles exist.
